OneTrans-GSR：一种生成式列表排序范式，用单一自回归Transformer统一重排与混合排序

9/10
OneTrans-GSR: A Generative Slate Ranking Paradigm Unifying Reranking and Mix-Ranking with One Autoregressive Transformer
Feng Cheng, Yu-Hsuan Huang, Fengyi Wang, Chaofan Zhou, Zhennan Zhu, Huan He, Bo ...
核心总结:

针对工业推荐中重排与混排依赖手工规则和低容量模型、难以利用更多数据和算力的问题，提出将两阶段统一为单一解码器自回归策略，用奖励模型评估完整曝光列表并引导强化学习优化列表级长期目标，解码时显式约束业务规则。

个性化推荐理由:

论文将重排与混排统一为端到端生成式学习问题，用单一自回归Transformer替换手工规则和专用小模型，直接命中重排/混排核心场景且具备可扩展性。

2026-11-03 11:42:02 | arXiv:2611.04720v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Industrial recommender systems allocate substantial computation to pointwise candidate scoring, yet separate reranking and mix-ranking stages still assemble the final exposure slate using hand-crafted rules and specialized low-capacity models. Mix-ranking combines product cards, short videos, livestreams, and advertising cards supplied by distinct upstream business channels. Although these rules and models encode valuable domain knowledge, they are inherently limited in leveraging additional data and computation. They constrain the combinatorial space of candidate slates and determine objective trade-offs through manual tuning. To enable scalable and effective slate generation, we reformulate reranking and mix-ranking as a unified learning problem whose performance can improve with computation. Specifically, we instantiate this formulation across the reranking and mix-ranking stages and propose OneTrans-GSR, a single decoder-only policy that extends our deployed OneTrans backbone to generate the exposure slate end-to-end. During training, a reward model evaluates complete slates and guides reinforcement learning toward slate-level and long-term objectives. During decoding, business constraints are explicitly enforced. Offline evaluation shows that slate quality consistently improves as reward-model and policy compute increase. Deployed in a large-scale industrial recommendation system, OneTrans-GSR mixes heterogeneous content types within a unified slate. In online A/B tests, it improves GMV/u by 2.63%, advertiser value by 2.06%, and repurchase rate by 2.03%, while reducing end-to-end latency across the reranking and mix-ranking stages by 13%. These results mark a paradigm shift in slate generation: moving beyond human-engineered heuristics toward a general learned policy creates a scalable path for further algorithmic optimization and compute-driven improvement.

ReuseGR：通过自适应前缀复用实现离线-在线解耦的生成式检索

9/10
ReuseGR: Offline--Online Decoupled Generative Retrieval via Adaptive Prefix Reuse
Xujing Wang, Yufeng Chen, Boxuan Zhang, Chao Wei, Cai Xu, Jie Zhao, Weiru Zhang,...
核心总结:

针对生成式检索大模型解码成本高、不同用户相同查询重复计算的问题，提出将查询级候选规划与请求级个性化补全解耦：离线7B规划器生成共享SID前缀集，在线0.5B模型结合用户历史选前缀并补全剩余token，通过ReuseCut确定缓存深度平衡复用与覆盖、Path KD迁移后缀打分知识、BudgetCut在预算内选分支；核心思想是用前缀复用与自适应缓存深度实现大模型计算跨请求共享下的个性化检索。

个性化推荐理由:

该论文提出离线-在线解耦的生成式检索框架，通过前缀复用与知识蒸馏降低大模型解码延迟，直接契合搜索/推荐场景中LLM高效部署的核心挑战。

2026-11-03 03:51:38 | arXiv:2611.04259v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Larger generative retrieval models can improve retrieval quality, but their decoding cost makes low-latency serving difficult. When different users submit the same query, generating complete Semantic IDs (SIDs) for every request repeats computation, while their interaction histories call for different results. The challenge is to reuse large-model computation across requests while keeping retrieval personalized. We propose \textbf{ReuseGR}, which separates query-level candidate planning from request-level personalized completion. A 7B offline planner generates SID prefix sets shared across requests with the same query. A 0.5B online model uses each request's history to select prefixes and generate the remaining tokens. ReuseCut chooses a cache depth for each query based on historical coverage, balancing computation reuse and candidate coverage. To strengthen online completion, we transfer 7B suffix-scoring knowledge to 0.5B through path-level knowledge distillation (Path KD). BudgetCut then selects scored branches to improve final top-100 recall under a fixed branch budget. Its selection policy is initialized by on-policy distillation (OPD) and refined through group-relative policy optimization (GRPO). On an industrial search-advertising dataset with approximately 2M query--item records and 3.38M catalog items, ReuseGR improves query-level HR@100 by 3.83 percentage points and reduces mean online latency by 38.9\% relative to an all-online 0.5B baseline when prefixes are prepared. It achieves HR@100 comparable to an all-online 7B baseline with 57.7\% lower online latency. A 14-day user-level online A/B test against the 0.5B baseline shows relative gains of 0.93\% in click-through rate and 1.05\% in revenue per thousand requests.

有意义的ID：面向推荐系统的语义ID实践教程

9/10
IDs That Mean Something: A Hands-On Tutorial on Semantic IDs for Recommender Systems
Roger Zhe Li, Aghiles Salah, Mete Sertkan, Changhong Jin, Yingjie Niu, Ruihai Do...
核心总结:

核心问题：传统基于无意义整数ID的推荐模型存在嵌入表爆炸和场景专用架构的局限；核心思想：将语义信息（内容、层级、关系）直接编码进物品身份形成Semantic ID，实现大规模语义信息的原则性编码，以统一多模态、跨域和跨任务的生成与判别应用。

个性化推荐理由:

该教程聚焦Semantic ID这一RecSys核心身份表示技术，系统梳理其演进、多模态/跨域应用与实践，直接命中RecSys核心进展与异构数据统一建模方向。

2026-11-03 01:33:07 | arXiv:2611.04140v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Traditional recommender systems rely on arbitrary integer IDs to identify items, which carry no semantic information and force models to learn representations from interaction data. While leveraging content information can partially mitigate cold-start, this paradigm inherently suffers from exploding embedding tables and scenario-specific architectures. Recent years have seen the emergence of semantic IDs, which encode semantic information directly into the item identity, reflecting content, hierarchy, and relationships. This framework addresses the above limitations by offering a principled way to encode large-scale semantic information into item identity, benefiting both generative and discriminative applications and unlocking more native unification across modalities, domains, and tasks. This tutorial traces the evolution from raw IDs to semantic IDs as a scalable means of information utilization, offering a comprehensive review of their development and applications across multi-modality, cold-start, and cross-domain recommendation. Hands-on exercises guide attendees through building and evaluating semantic IDs, training models, and exploring cross-domain transfer. We conclude by discussing open challenges, including dynamic semantic IDs and multi-stakeholder utility. All materials of the tutorial could be found at the official website https://semantic-id-tutorial.github.io.

HARP：面向基于LLM推荐的无训练双画像智能体通信

8/10
HARP: Training-Free Dual-Profile Agentic Communication for LLM-Based Recommendation
Zhen Tao, Xun Zhou, Xuhui Chen, Ziyue Qiao, Qingqiang Sun
核心总结:

针对LLM推荐中用户画像单向、被困于重排提示且预热后冻结的不对称问题，将推荐重构为BUILD-TRANSMIT-UNDERSTAND-SYNC四阶段通信，并以免训练三算子实例化：规则提取对称物品画像、在检索端消费的可分解画像级同质性通道、以及单次反思式上下文画像更新。

个性化推荐理由:

2026-11-03 18:24:20 | arXiv:2611.05319v1 |
cs.IRcs.AI
查看完整摘要
In LLM-based recommendation, memory acts as a user-level agent in a memory-augmented backbone. Yet users and items remain asymmetric: only the user is given a structured profile; it enters a bandwidth-limited reranker prompt; and it is frozen after warmup. We recast LLM-based recommendation as an agentic-communication process in four stages: BUILD a self-description, TRANSMIT it along the collaborative graph, UNDERSTAND the other side, and SYNC it from the next interaction. This formulation reveals three unresolved communication challenges: the item side never produces a comparable self-description (one-sided BUILD); profile-level signals enter only the bandwidth-limited reranker prompt rather than retrieval, where they would not compete for context tokens (prompt-trapped UNDERSTAND); and the user's description is frozen after warmup with no principled update rule (absent SYNC). We instantiate the missing stages as HARP (Homophily-aware Agentic Recommendation via dual Profiles), a training-free, three-operator addition to a frozen LLM backbone: a rule-extracted symmetric item profile (zero extra LLM calls), a channel-decomposable profile-level homophily H(phi_u, phi_i) consumed at retrieval time rather than inside the reranker prompt, and a single-pass reflective update realising in-context profile evolution without any gradient step. On four public benchmarks (Yelp, Books, MovieTV, Goodreads), HARP lifts Hit@1 over the strongest published baseline by 6.9%-24.0%, with family-wise Holm-corrected p < 1e-9 on every dataset. Ablations and a per-case attribution study show that the operators contribute complementary gains, with the retrieval-side gate as the main source; we also report the trade-off introduced by the reflective pass. We formulate agentic communication and provide a training-free instantiation. Code is available at https://github.com/Jove-Zhen/HARP.

SPADE：面向本地搜索的时空偏好对齐与多样性增强

8/10
SPADE: Spatiotemporal Preference Alignment and Diversity Enhancement for Local Search
Chenxu Wang, Wenxuan Yang, Yueqi Mou, Shuxian Bi, Biao Tang, Peng Yan

研究本地服务首页从数百万词表中开放集生成主动查询推荐的问题，核心思路是端到端联调生成空间（业务价值对齐的BPE保留长尾）、上下文表征（多尺度连续时空编码+软门控）、偏好分布（生产曝光-点击数据上的多维复合奖励DPO与自适应难负采样）和输出列表质量（Sigmoid多样性束搜索防止近重复）。

个性化推荐理由:

2026-11-03 10:50:31 | arXiv:2611.04662v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Proactive query recommendation aims to suggest relevant search queries to users on the homepage of local service apps. Unlike conventional recommendation over a fixed item set, this requires open-set generation from a vocabulary of millions, where outputs must simultaneously align with user preference, match fine-grained spatiotemporal context, cover long-tail intents, and provide diverse alternatives within a few candidate slots. Existing approaches address these axes in isolation: standard tokenization suppresses long-tail queries, coarse location encoding misses fine-grained geographic demand, generic preference optimization fails to align with production impression-click distributions, and standard beam search collapses to near-duplicate outputs. We present SPADE (Spatiotemporal Preference Alignment and Diversity Enhancement), an end-to-end generative framework that jointly optimizes the generation space, contextual representation, preference distribution, and output slate quality through four components: Objective-Aligned BPE (OA-BPE) aligns subword merging with business value signals to boost long-tail query generation probability; Spatio-Temporal Adaptive Representation (STAR) replaces coarse location tokens with continuous multi-scale encodings and soft gating for precise spatiotemporal grounding; MARS-DPO (Multi-dimensional Adaptive Reward Sampling DPO) combines cross-domain pre-training with preference alignment on production impression-click data via composite rewards and adaptive hard negative sampling; and Sigmoid Diversity Beam Search (SDBS) improves intent coverage without sacrificing recall. Offline experiments on large-scale production data show that SPADE outperforms the strongest generative baseline by +5.6% HR@5, -40.3% GeoC@5, and +1.9% Exec@5. A one-week online A/B test confirms production impact: +1.88% Module CTR, +0.177% Search Volume, and +0.105% Search DAU.

SourceFlow：面向基于流的推荐系统的协同源耦合

8/10
SourceFlow: Collaborative Source Coupling for Flow-Based Recommendation
Chenghao Wu, Yongqi Zhou, Jin Chen, Yifeng Zhou, Jinchang Zhu, Junwei Pan, Chao ...
核心总结:

基于流的协同过滤中，现有方法独立于目标历史采样源，缺乏用户特定源线索；本文提出SourceFlow，通过邻域耦合构造基于协同证据的源分布，并结合协同共识与拉伸恢复，训练时用协同证据预测缺失交互。

个性化推荐理由:

该论文针对基于流的推荐中源采样问题，提出将协同结构注入源分布本身，属于RecSys核心方法创新，与我的核心域进展高度相关。

2026-11-03 07:45:02 | arXiv:2611.04455v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Diffusion-based recommenders learn user preferences by denoising interaction histories, but the added noise can distort the original preference signals. Flow-based collaborative filtering (CF) instead learns a discrete path from a sampled source to the user's observed training interactions (the target history), enabling inference in a few steps. Existing flow-based CF methods sample sources using global item frequencies to reflect the sparsity and popularity of observed interactions. Each source is sampled independently of its target history, leaving the predictor without user-specific source cues for learning to reconstruct that history. We propose SourceFlow, which brings collaborative structure into the source distribution itself rather than adding further conditioning to the predictor. Like inference from observed histories, training uses collaborative evidence to predict missing interactions. Specifically, Neighborhood Coupling constructs displaced binary sources by replacing historical items with unobserved collaborative alternatives. Collaborative Consensus further refines replacement probabilities using evidence from other interactions of users who share the observed item. Stretched Restoration retains these replacements at late flow times during training, providing more opportunities to recover the original interactions. Experiments on five recommendation datasets show consistent improvements over competitive baselines, including a mean relative Recall@10 gain of 15.7% over the strongest flow-based baseline. Controlled experiments confirm that these gains depend on correctly pairing collaborative sources with their target histories, as reassigning the same source vectors across users reverses the gains. Code will be publicly released upon acceptance.

数据受限稠密检索的缩放定律

7/10
Scaling Laws For Data-Constrained Dense Retrieval
Bowen Fu, Jingtao Zhan
核心总结:

针对高质量标注数据有限且重复使用多轮的数据受限场景，研究稠密检索模型的最佳可达评估损失（MAEL）如何随模型规模与唯一样本数变化；核心发现是MAEL可由非嵌入参数和唯一样本数的乘性幂律准确描述，并推导出达到MAEL的训练步数缩放律及权重衰减的最优EMA时间尺度不变性，进而量化了增大预训练模型规模相对于增加域内唯一数据在微调计算效率上的优势（约1.46倍）。

个性化推荐理由:

研究数据受限下稠密检索的缩放律，直接关联搜索/推荐系统的核心效率与性能权衡，并为LLM在检索中的应用提供基础性指导。

2026-11-03 14:33:11 | arXiv:2611.04979v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Dense retrieval models are typically obtained by fine-tuning pretrained language models on supervised retrieval data, where high-quality annotations or interaction logs are often limited and reused across many epochs. This data-constrained regime differs from the abundant-data setting assumed by standard scaling laws, making it unclear how the best attainable retrieval performance changes with model size and unique fine-tuning data. We study this question through the minimum achievable evaluation loss (MAEL), defined as the lowest evaluation loss reached during training under fixed model-size and data-budget constraints. We first show that learning-rate choices strongly affect optimization trajectories but have little effect on the final attainable MAEL: after rescaling the optimization-step axis, learning curves collapse across learning rates, and CKA analysis shows that checkpoints reaching MAEL learn highly similar representations. We then fine-tune 35 BERT-family dense retrievers on MS MARCO subsets and find that MAEL is accurately described by a simple multiplicative power law over non-embedding parameters and unique samples, outperforming commonly used additive scaling formulations. We further derive a scaling law for the training step at which MAEL is reached and show that optimal weight decay follows an approximately invariant EMA timescale, yielding lower MAEL and stronger retrieval performance on both in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks. Finally, combining loss and step scaling suggests that, in our observed regime, increasing pretrained model size is approximately 1.46 times as fine-tuning-compute-efficient as increasing unique in-domain data for reducing MAEL.

学习生成面向推荐的排序解释：基于语言模型的方法

7/10
Learning to Rank Explanations for Recommendation with Language Models
Ben Kabongo, Vincent Guigue
核心总结:

问题：如何用语言模型实现个性化、可复现的推荐解释语句排序；核心方法：将用户历史与物品元数据编码为查询、候选解释编码为语句，联合多正例对比学习映射到共享嵌入空间做稠密检索。

个性化推荐理由:

将LLM用于推荐解释的个性化语句排序，属于LLM在推荐系统中的直接应用，且采用双塔稠密检索与多正例对比学习，对可解释推荐与文本排序有参考价值。

2026-11-03 12:15:07 | arXiv:2611.04775v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Recommender systems are ubiquitous on the Web, making understandable explanations essential for users. Although large language models (LLMs) increasingly generate natural-language justifications, evaluating their factual grounding and personalized relevance remains challenging. Statement-level ranking offers an alternative by selecting concise, review-derived explanations, enabling fine-grained and reproducible evaluation using established ranking metrics. However, existing statement ranking methods largely rely on collaborative representations, while semantic enhancements often fail to outperform simple user-history popularity baselines. Moreover, directly adapting language models for personalized statement ranking remains largely unexplored. In this work, we introduce LMStaR (Language Models for Statement Ranking), a fully text-based framework that formulates personalized explanation ranking as dense retrieval. A query encoder represents the user's interaction history and target-item metadata, while a statement encoder maps candidate explanations into a shared embedding space. Both encoders are jointly adapted through multi-positive contrastive learning to learn personalized relevance. Experiments on Amazon Beauty, Amazon Toys, and Yelp show that LMStaR achieves the highest NDCG@1 across all three domains, outperforming CLER by 1.06--8.25 points (+11.8--65.9%). It also leads top-five ranking on both Amazon datasets, although CLER remains stronger on Yelp. Task-specific adaptation improves all five evaluated backbones and transfers across domains without target-domain fine-tuning. Counterfactual analyses further reveal the importance of user-specific histories. These findings highlight the potential of task-adapted language models for personalized explanation ranking.

ZeroGR：面向生成式推荐的过滤优先约束解码

7/10
ZeroGR: Filtering-First Constrained Decoding for Generative Recommendation
Xiangrui Yang, Jiawei Yang, Liang Zeng, Daqian Ding, Ziqian Liu, Bing Tian, Chen...
核心总结:

生成式推荐中约束解码需保证生成序列对应有效商品，传统先打分后过滤浪费算力于无效候选；ZeroGR提出过滤优先的约束解码系统，通过步切片过滤索引、共享槽执行与混合投影降低GPU内存与执行开销，并采用布局引导流式构建加速索引刷新。

个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦生成式推荐的约束解码系统优化，属于推荐系统核心工程进展，与LLM/Transformer的推理效率及服务化落地直接相关，但方法本身非LLM架构创新。

2026-11-03 11:36:55 | arXiv:2611.04715v1 |
cs.DBcs.IR
查看完整摘要
Generative recommendation is gaining traction in production, retrieving items by generating their identifiers one token at a time. The generated sequences must identify relevant catalog items, so constrained decoding couples relevance scoring with validity filtering. Because most possible sequences are invalid, scoring before filtering wastes computation on candidates that will be discarded. Our evaluation shows that filtering first can avoid this work with negligible impact on recommendation quality. We present ZeroGR, a filtering-first constrained decoding system for generative recommendation. ZeroGR uses a step-sliced filtering index to reduce GPU memory use by tailoring data representations to each decoding step. Its execution engine adopts shared-slot execution to accelerate data exchange across stages and hybrid projection to adapt computation to workload patterns, thereby reducing execution overhead. During index refreshes, ZeroGR applies layout-guided streaming construction to speed up updates and limit serving interference. Extensive evaluation shows that ZeroGR accelerates the filtering and scoring pipeline by up to $9.9\times$ over representative baselines while reducing GPU index memory use and accelerating index reconstruction.

QueryFlow：具有自适应查询演化的多跳图检索

7/10
QueryFlow: Multi-Hop Graph Retrieval with Adaptive Query Evolution
Manzong Huang, Chenyang Bu, Yi He, Jiapu Wang, Xindong Wu
核心总结:

针对多跳RAG中固定查询图检索器无法随累积关系上下文调整查询条件、导致互补证据召回不足的问题，提出将证据表示与查询条件的协同演化统一建模，通过循环将图上下文的查询条件化摘要融入共享查询状态，动态控制边界输入与关系特征以指导后续传播。

个性化推荐理由:

多跳图检索与查询自适应演化的RAG方法，虽非模型范式创新，但对搜索/检索增强场景有直接借鉴价值。

2026-11-03 10:14:30 | arXiv:2611.04625v1 |
cs.IRcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Multi-hop retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) supports Web information seeking and fact verification by recovering complementary evidence across related pages within a limited retrieval budget. Scoring evidence independently against the original question can miss supporting units whose relevance emerges through intermediate facts. Iterative retrieval incorporates these facts into subsequent queries, while graph retrieval explicitly models relations among evidence units. Yet fixed-query graph retrievers update entity representations without adapting the query condition to accumulated relational context. We view multi-hop evidence recovery as the coupled evolution of evidence representations and query conditions, with accumulated context informing both what the retriever represents and how it searches for complementary evidence. QueryFlow realizes this perspective by recurrently integrating query-conditioned summaries of graph context into a shared query state, which controls boundary inputs and relation features for subsequent propagation. Across four representative multi-hop question answering (QA) datasets, QueryFlow achieves macro Recall@10 (R@10), Full Recall@10 (FR@10), and LLM-Acc gains of 4.98, 8.40, and 8.0 percentage points over representative baselines, respectively.

DualEvolve：推荐系统中机制与编排的双层自演化

7/10
DualEvolve: Dual-Level Self-Evolution of Mechanism and Orchestration for Recommendation
Tianqi Gao, Chenxu Wang, Wenxuan Yang, Yueqi Mou, Biao Tang, Ming Liu, Peng Yan
核心总结:

核心问题：如何让LLM智能体不仅优化推荐模型，还能递归自我改进其开发工作流本身。核心思路：提出双层自进化框架，内层用任务反馈修改模型与训练代码以提升推荐效果，外层对实验过程打分并据此更新工具与技能规则、调整编排策略，形成机制与编排协同进化的自改进循环。

个性化推荐理由:

研究LLM智能体自动进化推荐模型与开发流程，属直接LLM应用与核心RecSys自动化的交叉方向，但对搜索/推荐底层机制创新有限。

2026-11-03 07:32:10 | arXiv:2611.04439v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Recommender systems are widely used in e-commerce and content platforms, where improvement requires repeated model design, training optimization, and experimental validation. Large language model (LLM) agents increasingly automate this development cycle. Sustained autonomous improvement requires agents to improve both recommendation models and their development workflows. However, task-performance-driven approaches select updates mainly by candidates' final scores, without a separate optimization signal for experimentation quality. We therefore present DualEvolve, a dual-level framework that co-evolves recommendation mechanisms and agent orchestration toward recursive self-improvement. Inner evolution revises model and training code using task feedback to improve recommendation performance. Outer evolution scores the inner-loop experimental process, updates harness and skill rules using these scores and execution traces, and adjusts orchestration choices based on next-round scores to improve the quality of subsequent experiments. Experiments on MovieLens-1M and three Amazon Reviews 2023 subsets cover four recommendation methods and 16 settings. DualEvolve achieves higher test means than Base, OpenEvolve, and DeepEvolve in all 64 setting-metric comparisons, with macro-averaged relative gains over DeepEvolve of 7.6% (HR@5) and 9.0% (NDCG@5).

查看完整摘要
Semantic retrieval models aim to match documents to a query's "meaning", yet it remains unclear whether they successfully encode temporal relations between events in their ranking mechanisms. We investigate this question by studying timeline-constrained document retrieval, where a query is an ordered sequence of natural-language event statements while a relevant document should match those events in a compatible temporal progression. We then test whether widely used dense retrievers and neural rerankers capture such constraints, and whether explicitly preserving temporal order improves ranking, using a controlled setting over a novel focused evaluation collection. Experiments show that state-of-the-art semantic retrievers and rerankers provide only limited improvements over lexical retrieval, suggesting that their semantic matching capabilities do not reliably capture temporal constraints. We then compare a timeline-aware method with both semantic matching and standard rerankers and find that it yields substantial improvements. This demonstrates that considering temporal order can provide a strong ranking signal in this setting, suggesting future research on improving temporal modeling in semantic retrievers.

Minsoo Cheong, Sungjoo Yoo

2026-11-03 18:16:29 | arXiv:2611.05304v1 |
cs.LGcs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Looped transformers are a parameter-efficient alternative to standard transformers for language modeling. By reapplying the same layers across multiple loop steps, recent looped language models match the performance of non-looped models several times their size. While this architecture effectively reduces parameter memory, each loop step still stores its own keys and values, so the key-value (KV) cache grows linearly with the number of loop steps and becomes the main memory bottleneck. We observe that the KV cache of looped models is highly redundant along the loop axis and that the loop steps differ substantially in their sensitivity to quantization. Based on these observations, we propose $\textbf{LoopVQ}$, a KV cache vector quantization method for looped transformers that codes channels of multiple loop steps jointly, and fine-tunes the codebooks to match the output of the full-precision model. At 0.5 bits per element, LoopVQ outperforms existing KV cache vector quantization methods for standard LLMs on general, long chain-of-thought reasoning, and long-context retrieval tasks with Ouro and Huginn models. Its design is orthogonal to these methods, and combined with TaSQ, it retains much of the BF16 accuracy even at 0.25 bits per element. We also implement LoopVQ in SGLang, where it nearly halves the decode latency at a 32K-token context and raises the peak serving throughput by up to $4.3\times$ and $6.1\times$ on the 1.4B and 2.6B Ouro models compared to the BF16 baseline.

2026-11-03 17:18:33 | arXiv:2611.05234v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Looped transformers increase effective depth by repeatedly applying a shared set of transformer layers, offering a parameter-efficient way to scale computation. However, standard residual connections aggregate information across recurrent depth into a single stream, providing limited control over which intermediate representations from preceding layers should be reused. In this paper, we introduce ARLoop, a looped transformer architecture based on attention residuals, where the input of each layer is selectively aggregated through attention over model depth. Distinct from standard transformers, we find that looped transformers do not require access to the full residual history: sliding-window depth attention matches or outperforms full-history attention at larger recurrent depths. Across controlled experiments with multiple parameter scales (140M and 370M) and recurrent depths (R = 1 to 5), ARLoop consistently outperforms prior residual designs for looped transformers. More importantly, we further scale ARLoop to 0.6B and 1.2B parameters under a realistic LLM pretraining recipe, demonstrating that the gains transfer to realistic pretraining, with particularly strong improvements on math and coding tasks. Our 1.2B model, pretrained on only 0.5T tokens with three recurrences, outperforms Huginn-3.5B (the only fully-open looped LLM as of our release date) across all evaluated tasks and is competitive with similar-sized non-looped models such as Qwen3.5-2B despite being trained on substantially less data.

2026-11-03 04:46:30 | arXiv:2611.04313v1 |
cs.LGcs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
We introduce a training-free sparse attention method that builds a prompt-specific key index to accelerate both prefill and decoding. The method clusters the keys into large semantic blocks that group tokens from noncontiguous positions. Each query position selects blocks by scoring their centroids and attends to the original keys and values within them. Queries that select the same block are processed together, so prefill stays fast even though each query chooses its own blocks. Large blocks make centroid scoring and this grouped processing cheap, so each query can attend to more tokens in the same time. Larger blocks with a larger token budget can thus be both faster and more accurate on long-context tasks than smaller blocks with a smaller budget. We evaluate two hybrid LLMs, Qwen3.5-4B and 35B-A3B, on one NVIDIA H200. Including the cost of building and using the index, whole-model wall-clock speedups over dense attention are 2.3-2.4x for prefill and 1.7-2.0x for decoding at 512K tokens, and 2.9-3.0x for prefill at 1M. The 35B-A3B model stays close to dense attention on five RULER tasks through 1M, on LongBench-v2 overall, and on MRCR through 256K. The 4B model loses accuracy mainly on multi-key retrieval, yet outperforms dense Qwen3.5-2B, whose latency is similar at 512K, on all three benchmarks.

cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Why do language models fail on long inputs? The common answer is length. We show that, for retrieval, length is cheap and competition is not. On five open models (0.5B to 3B parameters, four families), neutral filler costs nothing: accuracy stays at 0.97 to 1.00, up to 32K tokens on Qwen2.5-1.5B. Statements in the format of the evidence, placed at the same positions, drop it to 0.16 to 0.33. A single statement that gives the queried key a rival value multiplies the loss by 9 to 110, and every additional rival adds to it. Inside the model, one quantity tracks the failure: the attention mass left on the evidence. Across 126,473 trials it explains up to 58% of the variance in loss; log length explains at most 8%. The masked-filler protocol behind the claim that length alone hurts reproduces on MMLU (11 to 18 points lost at 30K tokens), but fails a visible control in retrieval: masking costs 45 accuracy points at 32K tokens, while the same filler left visible costs none. In retrieval, context rot is a competition for attention. We release the probe and all 126K trial-level measurements.

Collaborative search enables users to address complex information needs jointly. Advances in large language models (LLMs) enable autonomous agents to support collaborative information seeking within shared communication spaces. 查看完整摘要
Composed Image Retrieval (CIR) aims to retrieve a target image given a reference image and a modification text. Although recent approaches leveraging Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have shown significant progress, we argue that the MLLM's compositional understanding remains underutilized. This stems from directly mapping composed queries into a retrieval embedding via contrastive supervision alone, without explicitly resolving the intended visual modifications. In this work, we introduce Factorized MLLM Embeddings (FactEmbed), which decouples compositional query comprehension from building discriminative embeddings. Rather than directly projecting the composed query into a single embedding without internal reasoning, FactEmbed employs a set of factorized latents that simulate the intended visual modifications via an intermediate target imagination objective. This process encourages the latents to ground into target semantics before forming discriminative representations for retrieval. Subsequently, to propagate these factorized representations throughout the model for retrieval, we optimize a factor-level contrastive loss while distilling the decomposed representations into a deeper attention layer of the MLLM. Finally, for efficient inference, we retain the [EOS] token to establish a global-level embedding on top of these factorized latents, while we exploit the mean-pooled factorized latents to rerank a short candidate list at negligible cost, as both are produced by the same forward pass. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that FactEmbed outperforms prior works across CIR benchmarks.

A Circuit for In-Context Learning
Andrew Gao, William Gao

2026-11-03 18:44:34 | arXiv:2611.05344v1 |
cs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
In-context learning (ICL) lets a pretrained language model perform a task specified by demonstrations in its prompt, with no parameter updates. With arbitrary labels the model must acquire a mapping it cannot have memorized, so the learning happens within a single forward pass. Prior work found candidate parts but not how they compose. We give the composition for few-shot sentence classification with arbitrary labels in three 3-4B-parameter transformers. The model implements a key-value memory over label-token positions: each label token's residual stream encodes the label's identity, and middle-layer attention heads write into it the class of the preceding demonstration as its key. At the query, upper-layer heads match the query's class against the stored keys and copy out the matching labels. An early-layer signal encoding whether any label has repeated gates this readout; in its absence the readers attend to the start of the prompt and no label is favored. A separate set of heads suppresses the most recently seen label. Each step is established causally by activation patching, head ablations against random-head baselines, and steering with the model's own signals. A softmax model of the readout with two constants per head, fit on one prompt distribution, predicts another. The computation is conserved across the three models, the named heads alone reproduce the behaviour, and a transformer trained from scratch grows the same parts. The circuit transfers to semantic concepts and to labels with meaning, and the same parts, with a causal gate, appear from 0.6B to 8B in one family and survive instruction tuning. ICL in this setting is a gated associative lookup built from a few identifiable component types, traced causally from demonstrations to prediction.

2026-11-03 02:55:53 | arXiv:2611.04215v1 |
cs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Tokenization is a fundamental component of modern language models: before a model sees data, its tokenizer has already determined how the data appears. We identify and challenge three myths in the tokenization research literature. Myth #1: Bits-Per-Byte (BPB) is agnostic to the tokenizer and/or the vocabulary size: BPB is tokens per byte times bits per token, and the two factors do not cancel; using untrained models, we show that BPB prefers some tokenizers over others: the model has learned nothing, but the tokenizer--model pair has. Myth #2: lower BPB means a better model: On untrained models, BPB strongly prefers some vocabulary sizes, even though accuracy remains at chance, and on real models trained on the same data, BPB correlates weakly with downstream performance. Myth #3: different "tokenizer-agnostic" metrics yield consistent results: The "unigram-normalized loss" is not tokenizer-agnostic; its reported correlation of 0.989 with BPB comes from a confounder and falls to 0.057 once fixed; from the same data the two metrics select different vocabularies at each model size. We present a tokenizer trilemma: no metric can score only the true distribution best, give the same predictions the same score, and tie all untrained models, so choosing a metric is choosing a research question. We conclude with a caution for reading the tokenization literature and a conjecture for future work.

LoopKV：面向循环Transformer服务的循环感知KV缓存压缩

LoopKV: Loop-Aware KV Cache Compression for Serving Looped Transformers
Tianle Zhou, Yang Zhang, Tianyu Wu, Zhuohan Wang, William Nixon, Juncheng Yang
个性化推荐理由:

该论文针对循环Transformer架构（如Universal Transformer中反复复用层）提出循环感知的KV缓存压缩方法，属于Transformer效率优化这一使能技术。在RecSys/Search/Ads中，循环Transformer可用于建模长用户序列或多步推理的排序模型，而KV缓存是长序列推理的核心瓶颈，该压缩技术有望降低延迟与显存开销，提升在线服务效率。

2026-11-03 01:28:57 | arXiv:2611.04137v1 |
cs.LGcs.CLcs.DC
查看完整摘要
Looped transformers apply a shared block several times per token to improve accuracy without adding parameters. For models such as Ouro that keep a separate key-value (KV) cache per loop, cache memory limits the batch size that amortizes repeated weight reads. Uniform-bit quantization and fixed rules per loop do not allocate precision according to each layer's sensitivity in each loop. Measurements on Ouro show that quantization sensitivity varies across (loop, layer) cells, and the sum of individual cell damages approximates the overall output damage. Sharing damage also varies by cell, with later loops generally tolerating sharing better. Skipping low-attention values to save reads produces errors that compound across loops. LoopKV uses a one-time offline profile to choose 4-bit storage, 2-bit storage or reuse of the final loop's KV for each cell. The planner minimizes reads under budgets for predicted damage and storage. During decoding, each loop reads the high 2 bits of each 4-bit value and fetches the low bits only where its own attention weights require 4-bit precision. A single fused kernel in vLLM executes the plan. Serving Ouro-1.4B on one RTX PRO 6000, the memory-optimized LoopKV-M plan stores 35% less KV than uniform 4-bit quantization (KIVI-4) on the same fused kernel and CUDA graph. It sustains 1.7-2.2x KIVI-4's goodput under a latency service-level objective with a 16K-token maximum length. None of its averages on six public benchmarks falls significantly below BF16's.

查看完整摘要
Large language models are deployed as long-horizon agents that compose diverse tools across MCP services into executable workflows. Existing MCP environment-scaling methods are data-centric: they first synthesize a database and scenario, then derive tools and tasks from them. As a result, tool interfaces may not match real ones, tool compositions are not grounded in execution, and tasks rarely span more than one environment. We propose ToolSpace, a tool-centric framework that reverses this order. ToolSpace mines data, state, and retry dependencies from adjacent calls in execution traces of real MCP services and open agent corpora, using deterministic rules rather than LLM judgment, and clusters the resulting weighted tool-composition graph into environments. Databases are synthesized only for state-mutating environments, while query and stateless tools replay recorded returns. ToolSpace then performs state-aware random walks with rollback and decompiles each chain into a natural-language task with assertion-based checks, keeping only samples that replay successfully from the initial state. Applied to AutomationBench and tau3-Bench Banking, the pipeline produces harder Pro versions on which 7 frontier models drop by up to 38.2 points. Across seven long-horizon tool-use benchmarks, ToolSpace raises Qwen3.6-35B-A3B from 23.0 to 41.0 average score (43.2 with RL) and GLM-5.2 from 44.6 to 56.6, on par with Claude Opus 5. Ablations show that execution-witnessed edges clearly outperform LLM-judged ones, and performance keeps improving with data and environment scale.

2026-11-03 07:39:19 | arXiv:2611.04449v1 |
cs.LGcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Among 291 surveyed open-weight language model repositories (280 distinct embedding tables), 52.6% of untied gated and hybrid checkpoints (20/38) contain reachable input embeddings with norms below $10^{-6}$, compared with 0% of weight-tied models. Such near-zero rows can arise during pretraining when unseen rare or template tokens receive zero input gradients while AdamW weight decay shrinks untied embeddings. In tested Transformer configurations, non-initial positions or a BOS token prevent this failure mode. In gated sequence models (Mamba, Gated DeltaNet, xLSTM), a near-zero token can amplify input-embedding gradients by more than 30 orders of magnitude (up to 35). At zero input, current-token readout factors (SiLU gates or linear queries) vanish, suppressing historical-state readout. Residual RMS values can then fall below the square root of the RMSNorm floor across consecutive layers, causing Pre-RMSNorm to amplify backward gradients by up to $10^3$ per layer. When a gradient entry exceeds $1.84 \times 10^{19}$, FP32 global-norm clipping in PyTorch, Hugging Face Trainer, and FSDP2 can overflow to infinity and zero the current batch's gradients. In Telugu continual pretraining of Nemotron-3-Nano-4B, this discards updates on approximately 20% of steps and leaves triggered rows frozen. We propose two remedies. For released checkpoints, GroupClip decouples input-embedding clipping using a 64-bit or rescaled norm; its rescaled implementation eliminates update loss and heals triggered rows in one step with negligible measured end-to-end overhead. For pretraining, GateFloor ($\mathrm{SiLU}(z) + δ$, $δ\in [0.01, 0.1]$) keeps the gate positive at zero input, reducing near-zero gradients by 28 orders of magnitude in experiments up to 370M parameters, with perplexity comparable to the baseline.

However, existing unified multimodal datasets and benchmarks remain dominated by single-turn tasks. 2026-11-03 06:52:00 | arXiv:2611.04414v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Missing modality imputation aims to synthesize unobserved modalities from observed ones. Existing methods treat each modality as living on its own latent manifold, forcing the generator to learn a long, unstructured jump between them. We present SphereFlow, which takes a fundamentally different view: by projecting all modalities into a shared hyperspherical latent space via a frozen self-supervised encoder, cross-modal imputation reduces to short-range geometric transport between nearby points on the sphere. Realizing this idea with flow matching, however, exposes two geometric conflicts: the standard Gaussian noise source is catastrophically mismatched with the unit-norm data manifold, and the linear interpolation path departs the sphere surface into a region where the decoder has no training signal. We resolve both with simple, geometry-aware modifications: replacing the noise source with the observed-modality latent so that the velocity field learns only the cross-modal displacement, and training the decoder with a time-weighted loss that extends its domain to the near-surface region traversed by the flow. We prove that this data-as-source formulation reduces the worst-case off-manifold deviation from a quantity that grows with the latent dimension to a small, dimension-independent bound governed by inter-modality similarity. Experiments on BraTS multi-modal MRI imputation and CT-MRI translation demonstrate state-of-the-art generation fidelity across all settings, while downstream brain tumor segmentation with our imputed data closes roughly eighty percent of the gap to the full-modality oracle. Code is available at https://github.com/WTHH031230/SphereFlow.

Researchers struggle to find relevant datasets amid the growing volume of data across repositories and scholarly publications. Manual dataset retrieval is time-consuming and often fails to identify valuable resources that are not easily discoverable through direct links or popularity metrics. Dataset recommendation, which aims to identify datasets relevant to a paper, author, or query, has become essential for improving dataset discoverability and reuse. However, this task remains challenging because Scholarly Knowledge Graphs (SKGs) are large-scale, heterogeneous, dynamic, and incomplete. Although openly available SKGs organize scientific knowledge by linking scholarly outcomes, they often lack meaningful connections between datasets, leaving them isolated and underutilized. These graphs contain millions of evolving relations but still suffer from missing or inaccurate metadata, duplicate records, and weak dataset integration. This work presents the Multimodal Scholarly Attention Network (MM-SAN), a heterogeneous multimodal graph representation learning method for recommending datasets relevant to research publications within real-world SKGs. It operates in transductive, semi-inductive, and fully inductive settings, adapting to evolving SKGs without requiring retraining. Experiments show that MM-SAN outperforms state-of-the-art methods, providing a robust and scalable solution for dataset recommendation.

While conversational recommendation systems provide a more natural user interface, they face the challenge of progressively discovering a user's needs before they can recommend an item that satisfies them. To evaluate the ability of AI agents at this elicitation process, we introduce $τ$-Rec-Books, a benchmark that holds each user request fixed while varying only when the user reveals their requirements: all at the start (volunteer), one at the start and the rest when asked (mixed), or some never (hidden). A hidden requirement can be found only by proposing books and inferring it from the user's accept or reject verdicts. We generate 450 tasks over a catalog of 20,000 books from Wikidata, develop a rule-based simulated user to answer the agent, and score recommendations against catalog metadata without an LLM judge. Across four agents and two search interfaces, success rates fall for every agent and interface, on average from 0.975 (volunteer) to 0.756 (mixed) and 0.283 (hidden). Mixed tasks fail mainly because agents do not ask, and when told to ask, every agent reaches at least 0.931. On hidden tasks, most failures come from agents recommending a book they never proposed to the user. When told to propose first, agents only match a simple baseline that proposes popular books, and stay well below one that uses rejection to rule out possible requirements. We release the catalog pipeline, task generator, simulated user, validators, reference agents, and all agent traces at https://github.com/nbharaths/tau-rec/tree/main/data/tracks/books-wikidata-v2.

Direct Nash Optimization (DNO) aligns a language model to a general preference function by approximating the Nash equilibrium of a two-player preference game. Its practical variant, DNO-Prct, iterates on-policy sampling, LLM-based rating of the samples together with a teacher's gold response, large-margin preference-pair selection and a DPO-style contrastive update. We re-implement DNO-Prct independently and study it on two open-weight instruct models, Llama 3.1 8B Instruct and Mistral 7B Instruct v0.1, with GPT-4o or a larger open-weight model of the same family as the annotator. On AlpacaEval 2.0 with GPT-4o as judge, three iterations raise the length-controlled (LC) win rate against GPT-4o from 31.7% to 45.3% for Llama and from 6.8% to 18.4% for Mistral, and the tuned 8B model scores above Llama 3.1 70B Instruct on every reported win rate. With same-family open-weight annotators, the runs retain 62% (Llama) and 82% (Mistral) of the LC gain achieved with GPT-4o as annotator, so the rating step does not require a proprietary model. In a separate ablation configuration, variants that remove the gold responses, remove the data look-back, or accept only teacher-preferred pairs score 6 to 10 LC points below the full procedure, whereas varying the number of on-policy samples per prompt (1 to 7) moves the LC win rate by at most 2.1 points, and a margin sweep (1 to 5) run in its own pipeline by at most 2.4 points. We also report the cost of the pipeline and where the practical algorithm departs from the theory it approximates. All results are single runs, and GPT-4o is both the teacher and the evaluation judge; we discuss what this implies for the reported gains.

cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Skill memories encode past experiences as textual prompts to improve reinforcement learning (RL) for self-evolving agents. Complementing RL, On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD) provides dense, fine-grained supervision to internalize knowledge from skill-guided trajectories and accelerate policy learning. However, self-generated memories often contain noisy supervision and functionally entangled, redundant, or conflicting knowledge, making direct distillation difficult. Under limited training budgets, these issues can hinder knowledge internalization and even degrade performance. To address this problem, we propose MDSKILL, a multi-view distillation approach for skill memory internalization. MDSKILL organizes skill memories into three complementary functional views (state grounding, action planning, and execution control) and selects high-quality skills using dynamic metrics to construct view-specific and integrated reasoning branches. OPSD then transfers and consolidates complementary experiential knowledge across these branches into a shared policy, enabling efficient skill internalization and continuous agent self-evolution. We evaluate MDSKILL on three agent benchmarks against RL, skill-augmented, and self-distillation baselines that do not rely on stronger external teacher models. Without additional computational overhead, MDSKILL improves upon SOTA performance by up to 7.1% and 8.1% on ALFWorld and WebShop. Extensive ablations and evolution analyses further validate its effectiveness in knowledge internalization and policy improvement. The code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/MDSKILL-CF53.

We use the term representation validity to examine how text, audio, video, and combined inputs affect measurement and subsequent analysis. We compare input representations across six open-weight MLLMs and three human-annotated video datasets. No representation consistently performs best. On seven news-related constructs, native-video performance varies little across models (macro-F1 = .70-.77), while extracted-text performance ranges from .52 to .80. Input representation therefore requires validation for both the construct being measured and the quantity estimated from the resulting labels.

乱序OPSD：语义一致性何时损害在线策略自蒸馏？

Shuffled OPSD: When Semantic Consistency Hurts On-Policy Self-Distillation?
Qingyuan Zheng, Zhihao Dou
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究在线策略自蒸馏（OPSD）中语义一致性反而有害的现象，属于LLM蒸馏/训练技术的基础性研究。虽然其改进思路可能间接用于推荐或搜索场景中的模型压缩与蒸馏，但标题未体现与RecSys/Search/Ads的直接关联，且内容偏重LLM训练理论，故相关性较低。

2026-11-03 14:17:20 | arXiv:2611.04958v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Knowledge distillation improves a student model's reasoning ability through supervision from a teacher. On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) uses the same model as both teacher and student under different contexts: the student generates reasoning trajectories from the question alone, while the teacher additionally receives a complete worked example and provides token-level supervision along those trajectories. Because the problem statement is shared, its content and ordering shape both student generation and teacher feedback. Preserving its semantic structure therefore seems natural, but does it always improve learning? We introduce Shuffled OPSD, which shuffles the words of the shared problem statement while keeping the teacher-only worked example intact. Across three mathematical reasoning benchmarks and three Qwen3 model scales, the method improves mean accuracy over both an intact-target control and Standard OPSD under matched training configurations. The largest gain over Standard OPSD is 3.52 percentage points on Qwen3-1.7B. Further controls show that preserving larger word blocks offers no advantage over word-level shuffling at the evaluated scales, whereas shuffling the teacher-only worked example reduces accuracy at every scale. Cross-problem mixing and target-removal experiments further distinguish the roles of problem content, word order, and teacher context. These results show that disrupting the shared problem statement can improve learning, while disrupting the teacher-only reference hurts performance. They challenge semantic coherence as a universally beneficial principle for OPSD and motivate closer study of how input structure shapes student generation and teacher supervision.

查看完整摘要
Large vision-language models (LVLMs) can process interleaved text and multiple images in a single context, but how they internally identify the image relevant to a question remains poorly understood. We study this grounding step as in-context image retrieval and introduce ICIR-MCQ, a controlled probe for head-level analysis of image retrieval. Using each head's attention mass over image-token spans, we first identify sparse pointer heads that reliably point to the target image. These heads transfer beyond the probe: a single pointer head is competitive with external vision-language retrievers on a naturalistic multi-image retrieval benchmark, showing that LVLMs contain a strong internal image retrieval signal. Attention alone, however, shows where a head reads from, not what its output passes on to later layers, so we introduce a complementary output-side analysis. For each head, we train a lightweight image retriever on its value-weighted output and use it to identify writer heads, whose outputs contain enough information to identify the target image. Surprisingly, the pointer and writer criteria select substantially different head sets, with only 35 to 47 heads overlapping among the top-100 heads under each criterion. Targeted set-partition ablations show that the heads most important for standard multi-image inference are concentrated in this pointer-writer intersection, while heads selected by only one criterion show less consistent separation from random-head controls. The most consistent causal effects arise from heads selected by both the attention-based pointer and output-side writer criteria. Code and data: https://github.com/parkquasar/Image-Retrieval-Heads.

TraceRouter++: Causal Semantic Routing Realignment via Optimal Transport

cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Despite their remarkable capabilities, large foundation models (LFMs) remain susceptible to adversarial manipulation. Current safety intervention methods primarily rely on the \textit{locality hypothesis}, rigidly suppressing isolated neurons or features. However, harmful semantics manifest as distributed, cross-layer circuits, rendering localized ablations both brittle against complex jailbreaks and detrimental to general utility. To bridge this gap, we propose TraceRouter++, a routing-level safety intervention framework that explicitly traces safety-relevant causal routing pathways activated under harmful inputs and realigns their unsafe activation states. Our framework operates in a \textit{Discover-Trace-Realign} paradigm: it first pinpoints a sensitive onset layer via attention divergence and disentangles dense activations into interpretable routing features using sparse autoencoders (SAEs). Subsequently, it maps these features to downstream causal circuits via feature influence scores (FIS). Crucially, departing from rigid suppression paradigms, TraceRouter++ introduces a constrained optimal transport (OT)-based soft routing realignment. By formulating safety intervention as a tractable coordinate-wise marginal optimal transport approximation, our method redirects unsafe activation marginals toward safe-reference marginals within causally identified routing subspaces while preserving non-selected coordinates at the intervention point. This mechanism safely redirects malicious semantic flows while leaving orthogonal computational routes intact. Extensive experiments across representative large language models (LLMs), multimodal large language models (MLLMs), and diffusion models (DMs) show that TraceRouter++ outperforms evaluated baselines, improving adversarial robustness while maintaining a favorable safety-utility trade-off. WARNING: This paper contains unsafe model responses.

查看完整摘要
Large reasoning models achieve strong performance on challenging tasks, but their long chain-of-thought often incurs substantial inference costs by continuing to deliberate after sufficient information has been obtained. Existing reasoning compression methods primarily operate on reasoning trajectories, paying less attention to the local decision of whether further reasoning is necessary given the current state. We propose LoCo, a local preference learning method for reasoning compression. LoCo detects candidate overthinking states from the model's own traces, freezes the corresponding reasoning prefixes, regenerates shorter verified continuations from the same states, and trains on the resulting state-conditioned preference pairs with DPO. This formulation teaches the model when to continue reasoning and when to conclude, rather than simply encouraging shorter trajectories overall. We construct training data entirely from self-generated mathematical traces and evaluate LoCo on five mathematical and three code generation benchmarks. LoCo reduces completion length by 41.7\%--45.4\% on mathematics and 23.4\%--36.3\% on code, while maintaining or improving accuracy and achieving the best AUC across all code benchmarks. LoCo consistently improves the accuracy-efficiency trade-off on mathematics and transfers effectively to code generation despite math-only training, demonstrating the cross-domain generality of local preference learning for reasoning compression. The code and model will be publicly released at https://github.com/MuyaoYuan/LoCo.

语言模型的稀疏内部控制

Sparse Internal Control of Language Models
Xizhe Zhang
个性化推荐理由:

2026-11-03 05:33:27 | arXiv:2611.04349v1 |
cs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Language models are increasingly used in text generation, decision support, and automated interaction, creating a need for localized, reversible, and selective control of their behavior. Activation steering demonstrates that internal interventions can alter the behavior of frozen models, but how to select and evaluate compact, multi-site control interfaces remains an open question. I introduce sparse internal control, a framework for steering target behavior by applying inference-time interventions to a small set of internal model nodes. A local target-controllability formulation characterizes candidate site--direction pairs by their behavioral effects and specifies constraints on target recovery, off-target preservation, and intervention energy. Two complementary selectors instantiate this formulation: Driver-OMP selects nodes for sparse reconstruction of a desired behavioral displacement, while Coverage-Driver favors stable coverage across prompts. I propose a six-axis control-evaluation protocol measuring reachability, signed reversal, dose response, feedback controllability, off-target preservation, and held-out reuse. Exploratory experiments on indirect object identification, MMLU multiple-choice question answering, sentiment steering, and refusal steering provide evidence for the feasibility of steering task-defined behavior through sparse internal interventions. The six-axis evaluation characterizes these interventions beyond target movement, revealing distinct control properties that depend on the task and available direction information. Together, the formulation, reference algorithms, and evaluation protocol provide a foundation for the systematic study and development of internal control methods for language models.

cs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Small students degrade on large reasoning models' (LRMs') long solutions, so recent work shortens them by interpolating an LRM with a short-answer model at ratio $λ$. We ask what student weights retain when this teacher stops thinking, comparing LoRA adapters of three students from three teacher pairs at each $λ$. In each pair, the adapters' update subspace shifts most where the teacher stops thinking and solutions shorten most, locating the change without attributing it. We identify a shared adapter subspace tied to thinking-like behavior (reflective markers, monologue format and long solutions). Shared by adapters before that point, it keeps about half (post hoc) while one pair's teacher halves its solutions but keeps its think tag, and largely empties after thinking stops. Removing it largely turns this behavior off for every pair, student and benchmark. Only in that pair and one other student does it leave clearly fewer markers than removing the update's top components; elsewhere, same-energy random directions often nearly match it. Grafted into a short-answer student at twice its weight, it turns early markers (a post hoc count) on, like the whole update, but the student rarely finishes. Our work identifies a weight subspace tied to distilled thinking-like behavior.

查看完整摘要
Large language models (LLMs) often generate responses longer than necessary, increasing inference latency and computational cost. While prompting can encourage conciseness, controlling output length without compromising response completeness remains challenging. Aggressive early stopping can reduce generation costs but risks terminating responses prematurely. We introduce MinimalLM, a training-free framework for reliable verbosity control that combines uncertainty-aware, length-dependent stop-token adjustment with structural completeness guardrails. MinimalLM separates the decision of when to stop from whether stopping is structurally appropriate, coordinating response-level instructions, decoding-time intervention, and completion checks without modifying model parameters or requiring auxiliary models. We evaluate MinimalLM across three open-source LLMs and 330 questions, producing 5,940 responses across baseline and ablation configurations. MinimalLM reduces generated tokens by 47.6%, accelerates individual responses by 19-68%, and eliminates structurally truncated outputs in the primary evaluation. Despite substantial token savings, it achieves an average quality score of 8.04/10, compared with 8.42/10 for unmodified models and 6.46/10 for length-matched truncation. Evaluations across diverse subject domains (such as healthcare, programming, law, and science) demonstrate robust efficiency gains, while ablations establish the complementary roles of decoding control and completeness constraints. Our findings highlight that efficient generation requires more than producing fewer tokens: it requires controlling when generation ends while preserving response integrity. MinimalLM offers a lightweight, training-free approach toward this goal.

Real-world problems span diverse domains, including program synthesis, symbolic reasoning, planning, and optimization, and often require fundamentally different solution paradigms. A central challenge for LLM-based reasoning is twofold: identifying an appropriate problem-solving approach and formulating it correctly. In this paper, we propose AlgoPilot, a cross-paradigm reasoning framework that enables adaptive selection of the solving approaches and structured problem formulation prior to execution. The AlgoPilot framework contains three components: 1) a SteerLM, trained via supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL), that adaptively selects the solving strategy (paradigm and modes) based on input problems, 2) the corresponding expert GuideLM, trained via SFT, that generates structured formulation guidance tailored to the selected strategy, and 3) an ExecutionLM that generates solutions conditioned on both the selected strategy and its formulation. Notably, we show that the learned steering and formulation modules can transfer across execution models, suggesting their potential to improve other LLMs. Project page: https://sites.google.com/view/algopilot.

Recent multimodal language models can reason directly about robot scenes, but it is unclear whether they need to repeatedly reinterpret raw visual observations at every step of an interaction. We propose shifting routine spatial reasoning from images to text and symbols through a 3D geometric abstraction that is refreshed locally from observations and read directly by the language models. 查看完整摘要
Aerial-Ground Person Re-Identification (AG-ReID) aims to match pedestrian images captured by unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) and ground-based surveillance cameras. The task remains highly challenging due to severe viewpoint discrepancies, frequent occlusions, and substantial domain gaps between aerial and ground imagery. Beyond these observable factors, existing methods often overlook two fundamental structural issues: cumulative positional drift during hierarchical feature extraction and semantic inconsistency across cross-view feature distributions. To address these challenges, we propose ProAlign, a Progressive Positional and Prototype-Guided Alignment Network for AG-ReID. ProAlign comprises two key components: a Layer-wise Progressive Positional Embedding (LPPE) module and a View-Aware Prototype Contrastive Learning (PCL) module. LPPE performs adaptive positional calibration throughout transformer layers by employing conditional positional encoding in shallow layers to mitigate local spatial distortions, while introducing learnable static positional embeddings in deeper layers to reinforce global semantic priors. Meanwhile, PCL maintains view-specific aerial and ground prototypes for each identity and enforces cross-view semantic consistency via a dual-prototype contrastive objective. Extensive experiments on CARGO and two real-world AG-ReID benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of ProAlign, achieving state-of-the-art overall retrieval performance on CARGO with improvements of 1.60% in Rank-1 accuracy and 5.01% in mAP over the strongest competing methods.

查看完整摘要
Complex visual question answering often requires vision-language models to think with images, i.e., actively acquire local visual evidence by zooming into informative regions of high-resolution images. Our error analysis shows that many failures in this setting originate from incorrect observation actions, where the model fails to localize key regions or obtain crops that contain sufficient evidence for answering. We further observe that bounding-box tokens exhibit substantially higher generation entropy than ordinary language tokens, indicating high uncertainty when the model decides where to look. However, existing reinforcement learning methods based on final-answer rewards provide only trajectory-level feedback, making it difficult to assign precise credit to the observation actions. We propose Observation Policy Optimization (OPO), an RL algorithm for optimizing observation actions in tool-augmented thinking with images. OPO consists of two core modules. First, Uncertainty-Guided Observation Branching (UOB) treats each image zoom-in tool call as an observation event and selectively branches at high-uncertainty, low-redundancy events during rollout, enabling efficient exploration of alternative cropping decisions and their resulting visual evidence. Second, Evidence-Localized Advantage Attribution (ELAA) compares sibling branches using local evidence-sufficiency rewards and assigns credit only to the corresponding observation-action tokens. By decoupling observation-action optimization from trajectory-level outcome supervision, OPO improves visual evidence acquisition and further enhances visual reasoning performance. Experiments show that OPO consistently improves Qwen3-VL models across scales and achieves competitive performance against larger models and tool-augmented baselines. Code is publicly available.

查看完整摘要
Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs) have achieved strong performance on many video understanding tasks, yet remain vulnerable to temporal hallucination in time-sensitive reasoning scenarios such as scene transition understanding and event ordering. Existing solutions either require costly retraining or introduce inefficient multi-pass inference and external supervision at test time. In this work, we show that temporal evidence is already encoded in Video-LLM hidden representations but is not fully exploited during standard inference. Motivated by this observation, we propose Single-Pass Orthogonal Decomposition (SPOD), a training-free framework that recovers suppressed temporal evidence through analytical hidden-state manipulation in a single forward pass. SPOD consists of two components: Frame Distinctiveness Analysis (FDA), which identifies salient frames from middle-layer hidden-state geometry, and Temporal Delta Subspace (TDS), which constructs orthogonal temporal and prior subspaces from weighted inter-frame hidden-state differences. By decomposing final hidden states into temporal, prior, and residual components and rescaling them independently, SPOD enhances dynamic information, suppresses static appearance bias, and preserves complementary information. Experiments on VidHalluc, EventHallusion, and VideoMMMU show that SPOD consistently outperforms strong inference-time baselines across temporal reasoning tasks and model architectures while retaining near-vanilla inference efficiency. These results suggest that improving temporal reasoning in Video-LLMs may depend not only on additional supervision, but also on recovering temporal evidence already present in their internal representations. Our code is available at https://github.com/VioAgnes/SPOD.

Existing approaches exploit pretrained video priors by decoding actions from latent representations or inferring them from generated videos. The underlying link is causal: robot actions produce changes in the scene, and video observations of these changes provide evidence for inferring the actions that produced them. We treat video and action as paired modalities linked by this causal relationship and learn their correspondence through a unified training framework designed to better exploit pretrained video priors. Multimodal perception is central to many computer vision applications, including robotics and autonomous driving, where different sensing modalities provide complementary cues. Self-supervised learning offers a scalable solution for exploiting large amounts of unlabeled multimodal data. However, existing methods either neglect modality-specific information by enforcing feature-space alignment between modalities or learn only global representations without preserving spatial structure. We propose DenseTwins, a multimodal self-supervised framework that learns dense representations by explicitly decoupling the dense feature space into shared and modality-specific components. The proposed approach combines dense cross-modal correspondences with redundancy-reduction objectives, enabling the model to capture both shared semantics and modality-specific cues within a structured dense embedding space. DenseTwins jointly optimizes an intra-modal objective across augmented views and a cross-modal objective that aligns shared features while decorrelating modality-specific information. We evaluate DenseTwins across multiple multimodal benchmarks and sensing setups, achieving state-of-the-art or competitive performance across multimodal self-supervised baselines. Code available at: https://github.com/CarloSgaravatti/DenseTwins.

Zihao Qi, Zhibo Zhang, Miaoxin Zhang, Jincheng Cai, Zengqiang Yan

cs.CVcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) often process hundreds or thousands of visual tokens, resulting in substantial computational and memory overhead during inference. Existing training-free pruning methods typically estimate token importance using language-model attention, visual attention, or feature similarity. However, these methods treat visual tokens with uniform criteria and overlook that tokens may serve different roles during visual encoding. In this work, we investigate the evolution of visual token roles in LVLMs and observe that tokens progressively transition from local detail carriers to globally referenced representatives as encoding proceeds. Based on this observation, we propose BtsPruner, a training-free role-aware visual token pruning framework that preserves complementary token roles under a fixed budget. Specifically, BtsPruner first identifies the token-role transition point based on the evolution of attention concentration. It then discovers globally referenced representatives from later encoding stages and recursively retrieves local detail tokens from earlier stages to recover complementary fine-grained evidence. Extensive experiments across LLaVA-1.5, LLaVA-NeXT, Qwen-VL, InternVL, and CogVLM demonstrate that BtsPruner achieves superior performance retention under aggressive token reduction. On LLaVA-1.5-7B, BtsPruner preserves 91.6% of the dense model's performance using only 32 of 576 visual tokens (5.6%). In the high-resolution LLaVA-NeXT-7B setting, it achieves up to a 5.95x CUDA-latency speedup.

2026-11-03 08:19:29 | arXiv:2611.04489v1 |
cs.CVcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Vision-language models such as CLIP enable zero-shot classification from arbitrary class names, but under test-time corruptions, their visual encoder projects distorted features onto incorrect textual anchors with high confidence: a phenomenon we term modality bias. Standard entropy minimization reinforces these overconfident errors, which creates a confirmation-bias loop and collapses the predicted label distribution. We propose an online test-time adaptation (TTA) method that breaks this loop at three levels. At the representation level, a frozen self-supervised backbone (e.g., DINO v2) is combined with CLIP through a cross-covariance logit ensemble. CLIP text prototypes are projected into the feature space of this text-free backbone, so the self-supervised branch classifies native image features and is less affected by the text bias of CLIP. At the optimization level, a gated test-time information-maximization objective pairs per-sample sharpening with batch-level diversity, while an adaptive gate excludes unreliable samples from the gradient. At the prediction level, an online logit adjustment corrects the residual class-distribution skew. CDIM outperforms the compared CLIP-based online TTA baselines on ImageNet-C, CIFAR10-C, and CIFAR100-C, and remains competitive in continual and mixed-domain TTA. Extensive analyses and ablation studies verify the contribution of each of its components. Our code is available at https://github.com/B1ueC1oud/CDIM

The advancement of multimodal learning is heavily constrained by the scarcity and high annotation cost of high-quality instruction data. While raw videos offer an abundant and dynamic source of visual knowledge, transforming them into precise instruction data remains highly challenging. To bridge this gap, we present VidUEU-Agent, an automated pipeline that synthesizes instruction data from raw videos for multimodal understanding, editing, and unified tasks. VidUEU-Agent follows a four-stage pipeline: it performs global perception to filter low-quality shots, mines keyframes and multimodal signals, constructs metadata and routes the metadata to synthesize instruction samples. Web systems infer user preferences from behavior, ratings, text, and LLM-generated profiles, yet explaining a proxy may reveal its measurement process rather than the preference of interest. We develop an audit framework distinguishing point identification, partial identification, and abstention relative to an explicit reference distribution. Under feature-dependent, arbitrarily signed proxy bias, a strict majority of uncontaminated proxy attributions lets the coordinatewise median recover latent-attribution support and direction and, with externally justified positive scales, magnitude. We prove this threshold sharp using response-valid indistinguishable worlds, characterize sharp minimax error and exact compatible-target sets under approximate cleanliness and scale uncertainty, and propagate clean-proxy attribution error. Compatible sets showed that the current proxy configuration rarely licensed strict-sign conclusions. Weak comparator calibration limits the Amazon evidence to fitted-surface alignment rather than latent-preference recovery.

Suppressing Misaligned Behaviors in Language Models, Durably
Luxi He, Pengcheng Jiang, Jifan Zhang, Jiawei Han, Danqi Chen
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于语言模型的行为对齐（alignment）与安全抑制，属于纯LLM中心的安全/对齐话题，与推荐、搜索、广告的排序或建模技术无直接关联。仅当错位行为抑制技术能被迁移用于约束推荐场景中的生成式LLM输出（如避免不当推荐理由）时才有微弱间接价值，因此相关性很低。

2026-11-03 18:54:08 | arXiv:2611.05358v1 |
cs.CLcs.AIcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Undesired model behaviors can emerge or re-emerge at any stage of training. Once observed, how effectively can we suppress them? To study this question, we first construct two model organisms--language models deliberately trained to exhibit a specific misalignment--that differ in what triggers the behavior. The narrow-trigger organism misbehaves only when the prompt contains a specific cue: it cheats on multiple-choice questions by following a planted hint that points to a wrong answer, and behaves normally otherwise. The broad-trigger organism has no such cue: after fine-tuning on insecure code, it gives harmful responses across a wide range of open-ended prompts. We then formalize post-hoc behavioral suppression and evaluate methods along three criteria: immediate suppression of the target behavior, durability under further training, and preservation of general capability. We compare standard methods--SFT, GRPO, and gradient-ascent unlearning--along these dimensions. Building on prior work on alignment shallowness, we hypothesize that more durable suppression requires the ability to recover from a wider range of misalignment states. We introduce Prefix-Expanded Adversarial Reinforcement Learning (PEARL), a GRPO variant that augments rollouts with continuations from intermediate states sampled along cached organism trajectories. Across both organism settings and two model families (Qwen3-4B, GPT-OSS-20B), PEARL achieves strong immediate suppression while preserving task accuracy, and remains durable under further fine-tuning on either the organism's data or unrelated data. It obtains the greatest benefit when the undesirable behavior is broad and cannot be addressed through targeted SFT. Together, our framework and method offer a step toward more durable post-hoc removal of undesired behaviors.

2026-11-03 18:52:44 | arXiv:2611.05355v1 |
quant-phcs.CLcs.LGphysics.optics
查看完整摘要
When a language model answers, we can usually only estimate why. We build a transformer in which every step is a Born measurement - attention $|\langle q|k\rangle|^4$ normalised over each row, a measured product in place of the feed-forward nonlinearity, and a Born readout. With its denominators held at their values for the input, each class probability is a polynomial in the word amplitudes, so its integrated-gradient evidence is computed by a finite quadrature with no quadrature error: the words' contributions sum to the class probability above its no-information share, to $10^{-11}$ or better. On e-SNLI natural language inference, with about 98k parameters outside the embedding, the model reaches $76.98 \pm 0.17$% test accuracy, level with a softmax transformer of the same size ($76.62 \pm 0.36$%). Deleting the word its evidence ranks first changes the answer in 45% of test pairs, out of the 53% any single deletion can change, against 32% for the softmax model explained the same way and 40% by its attention x gradient. Attention can look more like the words annotators highlighted, yet accounts for the answer less well: deleting the word it ranks first changes the Born Transformer's answer in 32% of pairs. The same weights compile exactly to optoelectronic optics and to qubits, and a coherent attention layer becomes one circuit equal to the model (852 CNOTs on 23 qubits). On IBM's ibm_kingston, reading only the attention overlaps by Hadamard tests, the device gave the model's answers on two short test pairs and kept or changed them under deletion as the model does; the evidence itself did not yet survive the device, and we work out the shots and calibration that measuring it on hardware would need.
