2026-11-03 18:07:27 | arXiv:2611.05292v1 |
cs.CLcs.AIcs.CY
查看完整摘要
Synthetic populations, large sets of simulated people, are increasingly used in place of real people to pretest surveys, simulate users and communities, and evaluate AI systems. Each simulated person is defined by a persona, a written profile of fixed traits, demographics, and attitudes that a language model is instructed to act out. Every such system assumes that a person's identity is a stable attribute that behaves the same way in every situation the simulation presents. We show that this assumption is not a harmless simplification. Three decades of personality research find the opposite. How a given person behaves varies more across contexts than people vary from one another. Drawing on identity economics, which describes how different situations activate different identities and norms within the same person, we separate behavior into three parts, which are stable traits, the identity a context calls up, and the situation itself. We then prove, under two stated assumptions, that any simulation built on fixed personas, no matter how detailed the personas or how large the underlying model, cannot reproduce the part of a population's behavior that is specific to it and its circumstances. We propose a new evaluation criterion based on this result and a person-in-context design that can meet it in principle. We test one version on a naturalistic Reddit personality corpus and on a published clinical-trial recruitment survey of a community with documented distrust of medical institutions. The design does produce context-dependent behavior when tested directly, but in neither setting does it fully match the real population, and we show where it falls short. The consequence for practice is a risk that synthetic-population evaluations misrepresent the groups whose behavior is most shaped by context, a distortion that richer personas cannot correct.

cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Language-subspace interventions aim to improve multilingual reasoning by suppressing language-related components in mid-layer activations. Using MATH and MGSM across four open-weight LLMs, we ask three questions: do estimated language subspaces capture translation-associated variation? Does projecting them out remove language information? And do they improve accuracy without compromising response language? For the first question, we build subspaces using the existing Mean-SVD construction and our DiffPCA. Both capture substantial translation-associated variation and show similar patterns across model depth. For the second, we project out each subspace and train linear probes to classify language from the projected activations. The probes still identify language almost perfectly, showing that neither subspace exhaustively captures language information. For the third, we evaluate several intervention configurations on Qwen2.5-7B, by varying subspace creation methods and intervention operators. We find that the intervention that most improves MGSM accuracy sacrifices response-language fidelity, with only one intervention improving joint accuracy--answers that are correct and in the target language--slightly, by 1.60 points. Interestingly, subtle changes in the prompt alone can reproduce the accuracy gains albeit with significant damage to language fidelity. We argue that causal interventions must be judged on more than accuracy, to fully account for language substitution trade-offs.

The Right Source Is Not Enough: Understanding History Interference in Speech-LLM Memory
HongJin Song, Weiqiao Shan, Runwu Shi, Yifei Wu, Yujin Wang, Jiale Luo, Jiasheng...

该论文研究语音大语言模型（Speech-LLM）中的记忆与历史干扰问题，属于语音与多模态LLM的鲁棒性分析，与推荐系统、搜索或广告的核心排序与建模问题关联很弱。虽然记忆/上下文干扰概念可类比于推荐中的序列建模，但论文聚焦语音输入模态与LLM内部记忆机制，缺乏对RecSys/Search/Ads的直接或可迁移的技术启示。

2026-11-03 16:11:16 | arXiv:2611.05124v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Speech large language models retain audio history to support multi-turn conversations. Yet remembering an event does not ensure that its information is used only when relevant. We investigate why irrelevant historical audio changes answers even when the queried event remains represented. Controlled experiments on MOSS-Audio and MiMo-Audio show that historical values direct errors toward specific competing answers, beyond a generic effect of context length. Causal interventions distinguish two paths. In open-ended question answering, most history-value errors disappear when subsequent computation cannot directly access the irrelevant source. In multiple-choice answering, many errors persist: early historical influence is carried by question-candidate states even after direct source access is blocked. Low-dimensional interventions further separate source-role information from answer-value information. Their effects peak at different depths, and changing the source-role signal does not eliminate the causal influence of competing values. On naturally occurring errors, strengthening the queried role and counteracting the historical value each improve answers; their joint intervention gives the greatest recovery. These findings support a failure of downstream evidence use beyond source identification alone: source-role information can influence a decision without exclusively controlling which historical values affect it. Reliable conversational memory therefore requires not only retention and retrieval, but also control over how retained information enters answer computation.

One Assistant, Many Memories: Benchmarking Real-World Group Assistance When Recall Is Not Enough

cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
The value of memory lies not only in recall but in using it to provide appropriate assistance. Recent benchmarks test memory use in personalisation and agentic tasks, but seldom evaluate assistance without fixed answers or integration across multiple participants. Group chats make both challenges more acute: an assistant must integrate information from multiple members, track revisions and conflicts over time, and produce one response for the group. Yet existing group memory benchmarks rely on synthetic conversations and inherit constrained evaluation from general memory benchmarks, focusing on recall against fixed answers or decisions over a closed set. We introduce GroupAssistBench, the first memory benchmark from real-world group conversations with a deployed group assistant, covering 80,299 messages in 50 groups and costing over $53K. Over the same histories, GroupAssistBench evaluates memory recall (L1) with reference answers and memory use (L2) with service requests without fixed answers across single, distributed, and conflicting memories (M1--M3). Requests are verified to require group history, memories are anchored to their sources, and L2 uses tailored rubrics with penalties derived from real user feedback. Across evaluations of frontier models with full context, retrieval methods, and memory systems, the strongest memory system rivals full context on recall but falls far behind on use, especially for distributed and conflicting memories. Together, two additions that relate retrieved memories and bind remembered facts as response constraints bring consistent gains, showing that group memory systems must improve use, not recall alone.

Feeling of Knowing in Large Language Models

2026-11-03 13:04:46 | arXiv:2611.04834v1 |
cs.AIcs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Post-training updates change what large language models (LLMs) can answer, but may leave their answerability judgments misaligned. We study this knowledge-judgment desynchronization through functional feeling of knowing (FoK), which supports selective generation, retrieval triggering, and model routing without implying subjective experience. We propose two trainable channels: a knowledge channel that changes answerability and a FoK channel that judges the current knowledge state. Three stages train them: on-policy supervised fine-tuning (OP-SFT) updates knowledge from corrected on-policy answers; knowledge-delta FoK training (K$Δ$-FT) adapts judgments across intermediate knowledge states; and FoK-guided policy optimization (FGPO) trains the subsequent answer or explanation. Factual-QA experiments, cross-dataset evaluation, and three sequential updates show that explicit FoK alignment improves judgment quality on updated knowledge. MMLU and ARC-Easy separately assess capability preservation.

Where Is the Truth When AI Agents Disagree? Dependence-Aware Belief Propagation for Unsupervised Truth Discovery (Extended Version)
Laure Berti-Equille

2026-11-03 12:40:19 | arXiv:2611.04805v1 |
cs.AIcs.CLcs.DBstat.ML
查看完整摘要
LLM agents often give conflicting answers to the same question, and many deployed agents are variants of one base model, so their errors are correlated. We study truth discovery among such agents: inferring the true answer and each agent's reliability without labels. Classical truth-discovery and crowdsourcing estimators reward an agreeing bloc and can fall below majority vote. We propose BP-TD, which adds per-agent decorrelation weights to mean-field variational inference on a one-coin Dawid-Skene model, so that agents sharing a base model count together as about one vote. Its first iteration is exactly a weighted vote; later iterations estimate agent reliability. With 13 local agents on MMLU, BP-TD beats majority vote without any labels (0.716 vs. 0.624 on 160 questions; 0.707 vs. 0.674 on 10,000) and ranks agents by their true accuracy, which classical estimators invert. The gain depends on the pool: over 2,852 sub-pools of four benchmarks, decorrelation helps when the largest correlated bloc is weaker than the other agents and hurts when it is the strongest, and we found no label-free statistic that tells the two cases apart. Labels change the picture quickly: with 20 labelled questions, selecting the single best agent is more accurate than every aggregator we tested on MMLU, ARC and TruthfulQA.

cs.CLcs.AI

Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) has become an effective approach for improving mathematical reasoning in large language models. However, a fundamental question remains: when a model fails to solve a problem, does it truly lack the underlying capability? In this work, we reveal that direct failure does not necessarily imply inability. Some reasoning failures can be recovered by exploring the model's own trajectories, exposing a recoverable capability frontier: a policy-dependent region where solutions are not reliably generated through direct decoding but can be elicited through trajectory-level search. Based on this observation, we introduce ReSI-Math, a framework that explores recoverable capability frontiers and converts recovered capabilities into data-efficient RLVR supervision. ReSI-Math uses Self-Judge-guided evolutionary search to identify recoverable failures under a fixed policy and adaptively selects frontier problems for sequential RLVR as the policy evolves. On Qwen3-VL-8B, correct-incorrect trajectory crossover produces 272 verifier-confirmed correct offspring from 621 attempts (43.8%). After three sequential one-shot RLVR stages, the MATH500 and WE-MATH average improves from 78.71 to 80.16. Under matched single-problem RLVR, random selection reaches only 53.31 versus 54.16 for frontier-guided Q1.

查看完整摘要
Identifying the primary diagnosis alone does not fully characterize a model's diagnosis-generation performance. We introduce CardioVascBench, a retrospective benchmark for prioritized diagnosis generation from real-world Chinese cardiovascular inpatient records. It contains 3,262 de-identified cases, with 2,174 for training and 1,088 for evaluation, linking clinical narratives and textual ECG reports to expert-verified, ordered discharge diagnoses standardized to ICD-10. An English translation of the evaluation set supports aligned cross-lingual comparison. Our evaluation protocol combines terminology normalization and rank-aware scoring to separately assess primary-diagnosis identification, reference-diagnosis coverage, and list precision. Evaluation across 36 systems shows that higher primary-diagnosis accuracy does not necessarily imply higher list precision against discharge references. We also provide CardioKG-Agent, a knowledge-enhanced baseline combining entity normalization, constrained graph retrieval, and guideline evidence. In a staged comparison of three baseline models, supervised fine-tuning plus the agent achieves a 73.5% higher mean composite score than fine-tuning alone, representing a system-level gain. These results measure retrospective agreement with recorded diagnoses; agreement between metric rankings and physician judgments remains to be validated. CardioVascBench supports research on diagnosis prioritization and knowledge-enhanced generation.

自特权微调

2/10
Self-Privileged Fine-Tuning
Yangrong Hu, Xueyu Zhou, Yue Wu, Jian Huang
个性化推荐理由:

2026-11-03 10:21:47 | arXiv:2611.04632v1 |
cs.LGcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) is a key component of language model post-training, enabling effective task adaptation and serving as an important foundation for subsequent reinforcement learning. Recent methods seek to improve SFT by introducing token-level weights into the training objective, where these weights are typically derived from a model's output distribution at each token position. To better understand how such weighting schemes affect SFT, we analyze their optimization objectives and theoretically characterize the target distributions they induce. Our analysis shows that dynamic weighting methods, such as Dynamic Fine-Tuning (DFT), induce a heavily sharpened distribution, whereas static weighting methods fit a target distribution proportional to the product of the data distribution and a frozen prior. These findings suggest that static weighting provides a more stable training target, while its effectiveness critically depends on the quality of the prior distribution. Building on this observation, we propose Self-Privileged Fine-Tuning (SPFT), which uses a frozen copy of the initial model conditioned on privileged information (such as ground truth for mathematical questions) as a prior to provide guidance for token-level weighting during fine-tuning. Through a Bayesian decomposition, we further show that SPFT reweights the standard static prior according to how informative each target token is for predicting the ground-truth outcome, thereby assigning larger weights to more predictive tokens. Experiments on mathematical reasoning and code generation show that SPFT improves over standard SFT and other baselines across the main settings.

ReListen：使用冻结音频语言模型在长达一小时的音频中进行免训练证据定位

ReListen: Training-Free Evidence Localization in Hour-Long Audio with Frozen Audio Language Models

2026-11-03 08:12:55 | arXiv:2611.04480v1 |
cs.CLcs.SDeess.AS
查看完整摘要
Audio language models are meant to answer questions from the audio itself, but an hour-long recording rarely fits in their context, and the answer often depends on a few seconds of it. Transcribing the recording first makes it fit but discards sounds and voices, while systems that return to the audio must be trained for this behavior. We propose ReListen, a training-free method in which a frozen audio language model finds its own evidence before answering. In a Jev-style scan, the model scores each 20-second window by the probability that it helps answer the question, and it then answers from the highest-scoring minute. This minute overlaps the annotated evidence far more often than transcript retrieval, for 88.5% of VoiceGiraffe questions. Without any change, the same scan also grounds events on LAT-Bench as well as a model trained for temporal grounding. Answering from it matches or exceeds hearing the whole recording with a small fraction of the audio, and on VoiceGiraffe it exceeds a transcript of the whole recording by 9.2 points. The selected evidence is also worth hearing rather than reading. Given the same excerpts, reading their transcript matches listening on speech content, but listening is clearly more accurate on questions about sounds and voices. Moreover, ReListen answers faster than every transcript-based system, and its memory stays constant even for recordings too long to hear whole on one GPU. Therefore, a frozen audio language model can search hour-long recordings by itself, without a transcript or additional training.

cs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Agile Earth observation satellites must coordinate imaging tasks within limited observation windows, while accounting for attitude maneuvering and onboard energy constraints. Competition among observation opportunities makes scheduling performance sensitive to the heuristic rules used for task selection. This letter proposes a large language model (LLM)-assisted adaptive heuristic search method for agile Earth observation satellite scheduling. The LLM generates an initial heuristic rule according to the characteristics of the scheduling instance. During the subsequent ant colony search, search performance and scheduling information are fed back to the LLM to refine the heuristic rule. The proposed modifications are evaluated through paired solution constructions before being incorporated into the search process. A unified scheduling procedure determines task selection, satellite assignment, and observation timing under the operational constraints. Experiments on six scheduling scenarios involving 50--200 imaging tasks demonstrate that the proposed method achieves 4.3\%--9.6\% higher mean observation benefit than the strongest baseline.

2026-11-03 07:25:42 | arXiv:2611.04434v1 |
cs.LGcs.CLcs.LO
查看完整摘要
Language models are accurate on most long-arithmetic problems, but their accuracy is estimated on samples and carries no guarantee for an unseen input; tool use is exact but moves the computation outside the network. We show that exactness at all input lengths becomes a finite, checkable property of a network when each of its steps reads only a bounded view of an external working memory. In Sketchpad, Qwen3.5-0.8B with a low-rank adapter executes arithmetic procedures on a rendered sheet. Each forward pass reads a three-cell view and emits one step: a digit, a carry or borrow, and the next operation. A fixed runtime moves the view and writes no digit of a result. We prove in Lean that the model is then exact on all inputs of any length if and only if it is exact on the finite set of views the runtime presents, that no finite test suffices when steps also read earlier steps, and that the guarantee tolerates bounded numerical perturbation and extends to compositions of procedures. All 2,446 views pass on the deployment device, a laptop GPU running the model in a web browser. The certified model performs signed addition and subtraction, long multiplication and division, square roots and greatest common divisors at arbitrary length. It solved 200 of 200 pre-registered problems in the browser, and 220 of 220 sums of up to 1,000 digits and products of up to 20 digits, of which GPT-6.1 Sol, the strongest of ten other models, solved 217. A symbolic runtime built on these procedures solves, expands and factors polynomials with every number computed by the model, and a multiplicative gate on the adapter recovers the base model exactly when the sheet is not in use.

2026-11-03 07:06:51 | arXiv:2611.04421v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Existing metalinguistic reasoning benchmarks require both inference of rules and their application, making failures hard to diagnose. We introduce typological feature induction (TFI) which targets the former step. Given sentences in an unnamed language paired with translations in a known pivot language, models must predict a typological feature of the unnamed language. From Grambank and multi-parallel corpora, we automatically build evaluation questions over 195 languages. We find that LLMs identify 59--80% of these languages from only 16 sentence pairs; and misaligning the translations barely affects their accuracy, suggesting reliance on prior knowledge of the language. Enciphering text to prevent recognition increases the corpus' utility, but mainly for features where target and pivot languages agree. On languages which models cannot identify, neither open model outperforms a majority-value baseline. Since induction from raw text aligns with field linguists' use-case, this work indicates the models tested are not yet ready for this role. TFI offers a scalable path to track progress in this direction.

语言行为的极小极大理论

2/10
The Min-Max theory of language behavior
Hiram Ring
个性化推荐理由:

该标题指向一个关于语言行为的纯理论框架（极小极大分析），未提及与推荐系统、搜索或广告的任何关联，也未涉及LLM核心架构、Transformer效率或VLM多模态建模等使能技术。其理论性描述过于抽象，难以看出对RecSys/Search/Ads的实际应用潜力，因此相关性很低。

2026-11-03 06:44:55 | arXiv:2611.04406v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
The "Min-Max" theory of language behavior proposes that humans engage simultaneously in minimization of processing effort and maximization of information content, and that this is a primary driver in language evolution (Ring, 2025). This claim seeks to unify observations from language processing (Mahowald et al., 2013; Futrell et al., 2015; Yadav et al., 2020; Hawkins, 2014), interaction (Hörberg and Sjons, 2023; Tucker et al., 2025; Jaeger and Tily, 2011; Smith and Culbertson, 2025), and corpora (Levshina and Moran, 2021; Levshina, 2023; Newmeyer, 2017), which suggest that considerations of processing efficiency and information content are both implicated in human communication. The interaction between these two processes provides an explanation for human linguistic behavior at various levels (Linders and Louwerse, 2023) and predicts historical developments in language, i.e. related to word order (Ring, 2025). Given that the original formulation of the Min-Max theory is based on observational data, the current paper seeks to validate the theory mathematically. Through a series of experiments that use computational models to investigate the theory's predictions, pretrained and iterated learning models are queried with synthetic data. The experiments show that manipulating the variables of token length and the weight of information content in a tabula rasa model results in word order change, as predicted by the theory. The experiments also identify important differences between computational models and observations of human language data, suggesting that current transformer-based language models may not accurately represent psycholinguistic processes. The overall findings indicate that evolutionary trajectories in language are highly susceptible to minimization of processing and maximization of information content.

Text-to-SQL Models Make Choices Without Saying So
Yichen Xu, Shipei Lin, Chenhao Ma

2026-11-03 04:48:51 | arXiv:2611.04318v1 |
cs.DBcs.CL
查看完整摘要
A text-to-SQL answer can run, return a plausible number and still compute something other than what the analyst meant. This can happen when the request leaves a calculation choice open, such as whether to count rows or distinct entities, and the model settles it without saying so. Standard benchmarks cannot detect this, because they score each question against a single reference interpretation. We introduce SpecBridge, which tests such a choice by varying it alone: it poses three otherwise identical requests, one leaving the choice open and one per interpretation, and scores each answer by running it. The databases are real, and the choices are ones that made models fail on existing benchmarks. On 204 hand-validated tests, four language models usually compute each interpretation when asked for it, yet for the open request return both in only 15-58% of the tests where they can, often without mentioning the choice. The gap lies in reporting, not in computing. Benchmarks even penalize the alternative: on BIRD questions where such a choice changes the answer, over two in five of the answers marked wrong are right under the other interpretation. Because the gap lies in reporting, a system can narrow it without a stronger model: it first decides whether a request leaves a choice open, then answers each interpretation in its own call. On our tests and on AMBROSIA, an existing benchmark of ambiguous questions, this approach raises the share of open requests that get every interpretation by 29-63 points over the single answer that production assistants give, without lowering precision on clear requests. It also needs fewer user turns than asking a clarifying question.

2026-11-03 03:11:35 | arXiv:2611.04231v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Power-grid customer service increasingly relies on community social media, where customers describe outages, billing, and equipment faults in informal regional dialect. Such messages exhibit non-standard morphology, semantic drift, and out-of-vocabulary expressions that make manual lexicon construction costly and degrade conventional NLP tools. We propose a weak-supervision framework constructing a regional slang lexicon from curated dialect anchors and power-domain seed terms. Candidates are generated via Pointwise Mutual Information with soft-label bidirectional anchoring, retaining dialect-anchored terms while recording power-domain relevance as separate metadata, and are scored by combining Sentence-BERT semantic similarity with a jieba-based wordhood measure. Uncertainty-aware active learning with rule-based and retrieval-augmented LLM oracles is applied under three query strategies, and the resulting lexicon is evaluated by a higher-capability LLM calibrated against human expert annotations via Cohen's $κ$. On 11,177 real WeChat messages from Yuxi (Yunnan, China), the framework achieves an evaluator-estimated dialect precision of DP~$=$~1.0 with zero false positives. An ablation shows that soft-label layering retains 751\% more candidates than strict power-gating at identical precision, expanding the candidate pool from 41 to 349 terms. Human calibration ($n=46$) yields $κ_{\text{dialect}}=1.0$ and $κ_{\text{power}}=0.85$. The resulting 25--33-term lexicon, 4--5 of which are power-relevant, provides a preliminary resource for dialect-aware power-grid service NLP.

NLP evaluation reports routinely cite sliced performance differences, most prominently the intra-inter gap in document-level relation extraction, as capability evidence. We show that such gap readings are joint properties of models and evaluation contracts: scoring function, gold version, threshold, output format, and prompt language. With predictions frozen, a method ordering reverses under a gold-standard exchange (+0.0363, CI excluding zero; tie-level after), movement localizing where gold re-partitions: renames are gauge transformations (Delta=0; re-annotation +0.862). We formalize this as an evaluation leverage account centered on the rank-flip condition. Constructively, the audited evaluation reading (AER) is a contract-marginalized, tie-declared, seed-prescribed, gate-filtered procedure whose tie declarations cover every contract-induced flip on the declared WMT19 grids (14/14; held-out 73% vs. union-of-ties 89%), matching the best single metric contract at epsilon=0.10 equivalence. Evidence spans DocRE, HacRED, a UD probe, and released gold revisions; an alternative-reference-set replication extends it beyond our testbeds. Failed predictions retained as boundaries; a 5% violation gate flags unverifiable settings and bounds survivor-selection bias. We release EvalLever, a preregistered workflow with an amendment trail; on LLM-as-judge grids, the audited ensemble stabilizes every unstable pair (13-15 to zero; generic ensemble shrinkage: the preregistered correlation criterion failed, the companion stabilization criterion passed).

2026-11-03 00:17:03 | arXiv:2611.04098v1 |
cs.LGcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Routing benchmarks often judge a router against a hindsight oracle that counts a query as solved whenever any model's recorded answer is correct, but under stochastic decoding that oracle mixes two different objects: the clairvoyant ceiling of committing to one model per query and the premium of calling several models and keeping any correct answer. A companion study measured this premium at 3.5 percentage points (pp) for 11 small open models on MATH-500; how large it is once current reasoning models enter the pool was unknown. We measure it with a fixed-protocol, pre-committed generation on a single NVIDIA B300: all 500 MATH-500 problems, 30 seeded samples per problem, Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct and NVIDIA's Nemotron Nano 9B v2 and Nemotron Super 49B v1.5 (a NAS derivative of the same 70B) under vendor sampling, a 65,536-token cap, a pinned Math-Verify scorer, and an append-only attempt ledger, for 45,000 scored responses. The two reasoning models both reach 96.1% accuracy against 76.2% for the 70B baseline. The empirical product-coupling premium is 0.215-0.588 pp across all two- and three-model pools; the empirical plug-in selection headroom over always calling Nano is 1.27 pp; and resampling Nano three times gives practically the same empirical any-correct rate as calling all three models once. Only 31 responses reached the cap, 25 of them with exactly periodic tails, yet the capped responses account for up to 60.2% of a model's output-length-squared proxy, and long generations made a fourth planned model, Llama-3.1-8B, too slow to finish. The premium is far below the companion's 3.5 pp, but models, pool size and protocol changed together; an oracle gap should therefore be reported with the pool, the protocol, the samples per cell and the decoding budget that produced it.

Practitioners who compare systems with an LLM judge and have no human labels often check a conclusion by repeating it with a judge from another provider. We test how far that check can be trusted. In a preregistered study (with a disclosed post-registration reduction of the judge set), five judge models from three providers re-judged 4,000 Chatbot Arena battles (nine systems, 33 system pairs) that carry human votes, each in both response orders. Agreement with the human vote was low at the verdict level (median Cohen's kappa = 0.22), and system rankings did not survive a change of judge: Kendall's tau with the human ranking ranged from 0.17 to 0.89 (median 0.67), so the preregistered hypothesis that rankings remain stable was not supported. Conclusions replicated by two low-cost judges from different providers matched the human conclusion more often than single-judge conclusions (+0.10 precision), but the gain came from filtering out one poorly aligned judge, and replication involving that judge left a conclusion for only 35% of comparisons. Judges that agreed with humans reached a stable conclusion with no more verdicts than human votes required; the others failed to converge on 26-70% of system pairs at the sample sizes tested (up to 100 verdicts per pair). Disagreements with the human vote co-occurred across judges far more often than independence predicts, although a single human vote per battle cannot separate shared judge bias from voter noise.

查看完整摘要
Learning a universal action representation from video requires modeling dynamics that are consistent across different executions of the same action yet invariant to changes in embodiment and appearance. Existing latent action models infer actions from visual transitions, but their representations often entangle the underlying action with irrelevant visual variations, limiting transfer across embodiments. We introduce DreamX, which repurposes a pretrained video diffusion model as a universal latent action learner. Our approach extracts compact latent actions from the video model's rich spatiotemporal features, compressing each latent video frame by $\boldsymbol{300\times}$ ($\boldsymbol{1{,}200 \rightarrow 4}$ tokens). In addition, by leveraging the video model's generative prior, these latent actions capture action-relevant dynamics disentangled from visual appearance. The resulting latent space serves as a shared action interface across embodiments (human and robot), environments, and action parameterizations. On LARY-Bench, DreamX achieves state-of-the-art performance among latent action models. Moreover, actions encoded in one setting remain effective in another: the latent space supports any-to-any video transfer and zero-shot action transfer across diverse embodiments and environments. Furthermore, this shared interface also enables policy learning in a compact latent action space, reducing the computational cost of video-diffusion-based policies.

Reliable evaluation of visual 3D reconstruction and depth estimation in vegetation-rich outdoor environments is limited by the scarcity of geometric ground-truth data and the cost of complete scanner surveys. We present a LiDARbased reference-generation pipeline for evaluating the zero-shot performance of feed-forward visual-geometry models alongside conventional structure-from-motion (SfM) methods. The pipeline reuses camera images, LiDAR scans, and reference poses from existing multi-sensor localization datasets to generate per-view depth maps and visible reference point clouds. To address visibility errors in accumulated LiDAR scans, we introduce the LargeDepth-Gap Visibility Score (LDGVS) for point-wise visibility filtering and automatic scale selection in Hidden Point Removal(HPR). Experiments on TartanAirV2 and TartanGround show that, compared with Poisson surface reconstruction, which is an approach suited to completed scan surveys, the combined HPR and LDGVS filtering retains approximately three times the point density at comparable absolute relative error. We also apply the pipeline to the BotanicGarden dataset to systematically compare depth estimation and 3D reconstruction methods. Feedforward methods produce dense depth predictions efficiently, but their 3D reconstructions can contain duplicated structures. Conventional SfM generally produces more coherent geometry, but its reconstructions remain sparse and more prone to failure in challenging vegetated scenes. The benchmark toolkit and datasets will be released.

2026-11-03 18:07:52 | arXiv:2611.05294v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
A real photograph is often blurry, grainy, compressed, scratched, and low-resolution at the same time. Each defect has its own severity and its own place in the image. The corrections for these defects conflict with each other. Exist- ing models describe the whole image with one description, so they cannot say where each correction belongs. To solve this problem, we present Wonder 4, a mixture-of-experts (MoE) model for image quality enhancement. It corrects several defects of a real photograph at the same time, each in the region where it occurs. Every block of the model holds a mixture of N = 13 experts. A router, trained with the model, sends every image patch to k = 5 of them. No degradation classifier, mask, prompt, detector, or task label is used. Another issue is that standard MoE training, taken from language models, pushes every expert toward equal use. Equal use applies every correction everywhere, even where it is not needed. We replace this rule with a bias that only sets a minimum share of use. Across all patches, an expert used less than this minimum gets higher router scores until it is selected again. An expert above the minimum is not changed, so no expert is forced to be used as often as the others. The bias needs no extra loss. The method adds no extra module and no extra processing step. We evaluate Wonder 4 on DIV2K and Flickr2K, against five published methods and three open-weight pipelines. It reaches the best LPIPS, DISTS, and NIQE on all three test settings (DIV2K x4, DIV2K x8, and Flickr2K x4).

Visual place recognition (VPR) localises a camera by retrieving similar geo-tagged images, and current foundation-model VPR models are accurate across cities, seasons and illumination, but not robust to adversarial perturbations: under a white-box attack on the ranking at an imperceptible budget of 1/255, SuperVLAD and BoQ keep at most 0.2% top-1 recall (R@1) on four benchmarks. We propose anchored rank-adversarial fine-tuning (ARAFT), which fine-tunes a released model with a listwise rank loss on each adversarial query's hardest positive and an anchor to the clean descriptor of the frozen pretrained model. Checkpoints are selected under a clean-accuracy budget fixed in advance, and the full trade-off is reported. Within five validation R@1 points of clean cost, plain adversarial training has no admissible checkpoint, whereas ARAFT raises robust R@1 from zero to 47.3 on SuperVLAD and 55.3 on BoQ; plain adversarial training exceeds this only at about three times the clean cost. Against unbudgeted plain adversarial training on the test sets, ARAFT matches its robust R@1 at 2/255 with about ten more clean R@1 points. At 4/255, plain adversarial training is 6-9 robust R@1 points ahead, while ARAFT still keeps its ten-point clean advantage. Developed on SuperVLAD, ARAFT transfers to BoQ without re-tuning, and its robustness holds against stronger optimisers, with no sign of gradient masking.

We aim to build a truly unified motion model that can understand, generate, and edit human motion within a single framework. Existing motion UMMs remain only partially unified: motion understanding is handled by a language model, while generation is often delegated to a separate diffusion module operating over learned motion latents. We introduce Möbius, which brings the entire diffusion process into the same multimodal representation space used for motion understanding. 2026-11-03 16:31:34 | arXiv:2611.05158v1 |
cs.CVcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Vision-language verifiers judge whether an image faithfully matches a textual description, yet existing training pipelines typically rely on static supervision from stronger external judges or curated hard-negative data. Such supervision requires external generation or annotation, is teacher-bounded, and is difficult to adapt as model failures evolve. We introduce PROVE (Perturb-and-Repair Optimization for Vision-Language Evaluation), a self-play framework that trains verifiers through a closed perturb-verify-repair-verify loop. Starting from a known-correct image-caption pair, a Mutator introduces a typed perturbation over objects, attributes, counts, or relations. The verifier then judges the perturbed caption and provides textual feedback, which a Repairer uses to recover a faithful caption for re-verification. These two verification rounds produce structured rewards for informative mismatches and visually grounded repairs. Since perturbation, verification, and repair are role-conditioned behaviors of the same evolving multimodal backbone, updating the shared parameters also improves the verifier itself. Experiments show that PROVE improves fine-grained compositional verification across model scales and families and strengthens visual-detail robustness on the reference backbone, with limited, benchmark-dependent changes on broader multimodal tasks.

cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Estimating semantic correspondences between different object instances of similar categories in real-world images is a fundamental challenge in computer vision. While the recent progress in foundation models has significantly advanced solutions for such difficult image correspondence problems, respective methods are often insufficiently regularised. For example, common nearest-neighbour matching with foundation model features often results in noisy correspondences, which is particularly prominent when solving for dense correspondences. In this work, we tackle image matching via functional maps, which have been popularised for 3D shape matching due to their powerful spectral formalism utilising an efficient low-dimensional linear representation. However, functional maps require that domains (in our cases the rectangular image grids) are equipped with an informative and non-trivial geometry. To define a meaningful manifold structure for images, we use Galerkin's method from finite elements to derive a discrete Laplace-Beltrami operator (LBO) based on a manifold embedding of deep image features. Further, we leverage a neural adjoint map that allows to represent non-linear mappings. We demonstrate that our method sets the new state of the art among zero-shot image correspondence methods on multiple dense correspondence benchmarks of real-world images. Our code is publicly available: https://github.com/NafieAmrani/NAMI

VIGOR: Visual Gain Ordering for Hallucination Mitigation in Multimodal Discrete Diffusion Language Models
Junzhe Chen, Tian Qin, Eugenie Shi, Tianshu Zhang, Qiang Ju, Lijie Wen

2026-11-03 15:28:11 | arXiv:2611.05066v1 |
cs.CVcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Multimodal discrete diffusion language models (dLLMs) generate responses through iterative mask prediction and offer a promising alternative to autoregressive large vision-language models. However, they still suffer from hallucination, producing outputs that are plausible under language priors but insufficiently grounded in the image. We argue that hallucination in multimodal dLLMs is not only a token prediction problem, but also a premature commitment problem: during iterative denoising, some masked positions are unmasked too early because their confidence is driven more by language priors than by visual evidence, and these early commitments can bias later generation. To address this issue, we propose Visual Gain Ordering (VIGOR), a training-free and plug-and-play inference strategy that reorders masked positions according to counterfactual visual evidence. At each denoising step, VIGOR compares the confidence of each candidate token under the original image-conditioned pass and an additional visual-ablation pass, and prioritizes positions whose predictions are more strongly supported by the image. Experiments on LLaDA-V and Lumina-DiMOO show that VIGOR consistently reduces hallucination and improves multimodal reasoning performance across benchmarks. Code is available at https://github.com/THU-BPM/Vigor.

An Agentic Framework for Quality-Aware and Scenario-Adaptive Image Compression

2026-11-03 14:19:39 | arXiv:2611.04960v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Despite decades of progress, image codecs remain difficult to adapt to diverse real-world compression scenarios for two main reasons. First, most image codecs are optimized for a specific objective and require substantial redevelopment when the compression goal changes. Second, they typically assume clean inputs, whereas sensor-captured images often contain degradations that alter rate-quality behavior. To address these challenges, we propose Practical Image Coding Agents (PICA), a multi-agent framework that adapts existing codecs to diverse user requirements and potentially degraded inputs. PICA consists of four collaborative agents: a Perception agent that interprets user intent and analyzes image content, an Improvement agent that jointly considers bitrate and reconstruction quality to guide pre-coding restoration, a Coding agent that refines quality-control settings from global to regional levels, and an After-coding improvement agent that addresses residual degradations and compression-induced artifacts through restoration guided solely by image quality. To enable codec-agnostic regional adaptation, we introduce Agentic ROI Coding (AROIC), which treats the codec as a black box and allows the agent to automatically schedule, execute, evaluate, and reflect ROI coding plans without architectural modifications or codec-specific retraining. We integrate these four agents with a Scenario-Guided Quality-Rate (SGQR) evaluation strategy to enable more effective scenario-adaptive compression across heterogeneous codecs. Comprehensive experiments on clean, noisy, and blurred images across standard, perceptual, and machine-vision compression scenarios demonstrate that PICA consistently enhances the compression performance of both the conventional H.266/VVC codec and the neural Cheng2020 codec, outperforming competing compression methods on the Kodak, CLIC2020, and HEVC datasets.

Lightweight graph transformers for clutter and target classification in automotive radar

2026-11-03 14:18:46 | arXiv:2611.04959v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Radar is a core sensor modality for scene perception to achieve higher levels of autonomy in automotive driving. A common occurrence in automotive radars is clutter - detections of non-existent moving objects, that can adversely impact target detection and classification performance and subsequent driving actions. We propose lightweight tiny graph transformer network (TGTNet) models for classifying clutter from stationary and moving targets in the scene. Performance evaluation on the public RadarScenes show that our proposed TGTNet models achieve similar classification performance in precision and recall metrics in comparison to state of the art models, with one to two orders of magnitude lower model size and significantly faster inference.

PATTS:面向多模态离散扩散大语言模型中思维-图像生成的并行感知测试时扩展

2/10
PATTS: Parallel-Aware Test-Time Scaling for Thought-Image Generation in Multimodal Discrete Diffusion LLMs
Yasong Dai, Zeeshan Hayder, David Ahmedt-Aristizabal, Hongdong Li
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究的是多模态离散扩散大语言模型中思维-图像生成的测试时扩展，属于面向多模态生成的纯LLM技术，没有展示出与推荐系统、搜索或广告的明确联系。测试时扩展与并行解码可能在提升推理效率方面有间接参考价值，但其生成导向的重点偏离了排序与检索任务，因此相关性较低。

2026-11-03 14:10:56 | arXiv:2611.04948v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Test-time scaling (TTS) improves generative models by allocating additional inference-time computation to sampling, search, and verification. Existing image generation approaches, however, typically rank completed images or image-centric intermediate states. We study TTS for parallel multimodal discrete diffusion language models, in which reasoning tokens and image tokens are decoded jointly within a single generation trajectory. We first diagnose a limitation of conventional final-image selection: on GenEval, a CLIP-style image-prompt score is weakly aligned with within-prompt compositional correctness, leaving substantial headroom between verifier selection and oracle selection. We then propose PATTS, a novel parallel-aware TTS framework that searches over intermediate thought-image states using cross-modal verifier signals. PATTS uses a state-dependent reliability weight, derived from model-internal trajectory diagnostics, to modulate the influence of external semantic verification during generation. In the fixed N=32 evaluation on MMaDA-Parallel, the PATTS reaches a GenEval score of 0.844, compared with 0.628 without TTS, 0.787 for Best-of-N, and 0.810 for FKD. These results show that reliability-aware trajectory verification can improve test-time selection for parallel thought-image co-generation under the evaluated search budget. Our code release is at https://github.com/Ringo-Star/PATTS.git

网络摄像头眼动追踪在“真实环境”中效果如何？Chiasm的大规模验证及与WebGazer的比较

2/10
How Well Does Webcam Eye Tracking Work "in the Wild"? A Large-Scale Validation of Chiasm and a Comparison with WebGazer
Artem V. Belopolsky, Florian Perdreau
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究的是基于网络摄像头的眼动追踪技术验证，属于人机交互与视觉感知领域，与LLM、Transformer架构、RecSys/Search/Ads的核心技术无关。虽然眼动追踪可能间接用于用户行为分析，但本文不涉及推荐、搜索或广告排序的任何建模或技术进展，因此相关性很低。

2026-11-03 14:10:44 | arXiv:2611.04946v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Webcam eye tracking can greatly accelerate data collection in basic and applied research by reaching large, diverse samples of participants online. However, its spatial performance is considered well below that of hardware-based systems, limiting its use. We evaluated the first-generation Chiasm webcam eye tracker in a large online study ($N = 505$) spanning diverse participants, devices, and recording conditions. Chiasm achieved a mean spatial accuracy of 2.39 cm ($\approx 2.28^\circ$ at 60 cm), spatial precision (SD) of 1.05 cm ($\approx 1.00^\circ$ at 60 cm), and sample-to-sample precision (RMS-S2S) of 1.29 cm ($\approx 1.23^\circ$ at 60 cm). For the 91% of participants who met a 3 cm calibration criterion, spatial accuracy was 2.15 cm ($\approx 2.05^\circ$ at 60 cm). After three minutes of a task that encouraged head movement, spatial accuracy declined by 0.59 cm, while the gaze center at each target shifted by a mean distance of only 0.37 cm. On the validation block, the offset was 0.30 cm larger for men than for women. Age, region of residence, face luminance, and Michelson contrast showed no reliable relationship with spatial accuracy. A higher eye aspect ratio, a more open eye, was associated with a smaller offset. Wearing glasses decreased spatial accuracy on every validation block. In a comparison between a random subsample ($N = 101$) and a separate sample tested with the open-source tracker WebGazer ($N = 101$) using an identical protocol, Chiasm showed better spatial accuracy (2.18 vs. 4.51 cm) and spatial precision (SD; 1.01 vs. 1.55 cm). WebGazer showed better sample-to-sample precision (RMS-S2S; 0.79 vs. 1.26 cm), which likely reflects conservative, over-smoothed estimates rather than superior stability. These results suggest that Chiasm is narrowing the gap between webcam and hardware-based eye tracking, making large-scale gaze research "in the wild" a reality.

VISTA: Vision-Language Integrated Semantic Triage Architecture for Online Face Clustering in Long Videos
Jiashu Liao, Joanne Garde-Hansen, Sanjay Sharma, Tanaya Guha

cs.CVcs.MM
查看完整摘要
Clustering faces by identity in long, continuous video streams is a challenging task that serves as a building block for human-centric video analysis. The majority of existing face clustering models are offline, i.e., they assume the entire data to be available altogether. In many practical scenarios, however, the entire data is either unavailable or poses computational challenges to be processed in its entirety. A handful of online methods that exist rely on geometric visual features and simple thresholding techniques, which fail when the faces exhibit significant appearance variations in long, unconstrained videos. To this end, we present VISTA, the first vision language framework for online face clustering in long videos. VISTA leverages semantic reasoning in a controlled, uncertainty aware fashion to maintain both high cluster purity and long range identity continuity. This makes it robust to the variations in illumination, pose, and costume changes that the faces and characters exhibit in long real world videos. Extensive experiments on the VPCD benchmark dataset (with 15 full episodes from The Big Bang Theory [2007-2019], Buffy the Vampire Slayer [1997-2003], and Sherlock [2010-2017]) demonstrate that VISTA achieves a robust precision and recall balance, consistently improving over the strongest matched online unsupervised baseline (COFC) under identical detector, embeddings, and evaluation protocol. Code and supplementary material: https://github.com/SkyJiashu/VISTA.

视频生成背后：来自可灵的关于多模态理解的观察与洞见

Behind Video Generation: Observations and Insights on Multimodal Understanding from Kling

该论文聚焦于视频生成模型（可灵）的多模态理解能力分析，本质上属于AIGC/视频生成领域，而非RecSys/Search/Ads场景。虽然多模态理解对VLM类比异构数据建模有一定启发，但论文主题是生成模型的观察分析，未涉及推荐/搜索/广告的排序、用户建模或特征融合方法，因此相关性很低。

2026-11-03 13:56:06 | arXiv:2611.04916v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Video generation has progressed from isolated clips to coherent short-form audiovisual sequences, and the bottleneck is shifting from rendering to semantic control. This report summarizes the observations and insights on multimodal understanding gained from successive generations of Kling. We argue that generation quality is the projection of multimodal understanding through the entire data--conditioning--alignment--evaluation chain, and we examine this chain module by module. Kling-MLLM, a shared multimodal foundation model that unifies image, video, audio, synchronized audio--video, and interleaved inputs, is specialized for each downstream module. Because a DiT is trained on far fewer training samples than a language model, we treat captioning as a semantic contract that maximizes description density, and extend it to reference-grounded captioning that makes reference bindings explicit. Prompt enhancement translates user intent into this caption distribution: basic PE is derived from captions of compelling videos and refined with text-level and generator-in-the-loop reinforcement learning, while agentic PE refines the story and retrieves compelling examples. We show that a reliable text encoder comes from perceptual training rather than from stronger language ability, and that candidates can be screened from text alone before end-to-end training. Reward modeling pairs a general reward model for explainable, localized critiques with a decision reward model for fast multidimensional scoring, treating perceptual bandwidth as a first-class design parameter. Finally, we review task-specific automatic evaluation, focusing on protocols that expose scoring shortcuts and help trace failures to their upstream source. We hope these lessons offer practical guidance for building the understanding stack behind controllable video generation.

Long instructional videos are increasingly used in medical education, industrial training, and procedural knowledge transfer, yet their evidence is often incomplete or unreliable: subtitles may be missing, ASR noisy, OCR sparse, and decisive actions briefly shown without narration. Existing VideoQA systems typically return labels or free-form text, making answer support difficult to inspect. We study Long Video Moment Question Answering (LVMQA), where a system returns timestamped answer-supporting evidence. We propose EP-LVMQA, an evidence-preserving coarse-to-fine framework comprising an Uncertainty-Aware Multimodal Evidence Timeline, a Coarse Evidence Planner, and an Evidence-Conditioned Fine Localizer. An evidence card transfers planner scores, matched cues, source-confidence proxies, modality-specific evidence, neighboring context, and a temporal prior between the latter two modules. On MedVidQA, EP-LVMQA achieves 26.1 R1@0.5 and 31.8 mIoU, improving the strongest non-ours results by 6.3 and 5.1 points. It raises Suff.@2 and Ver.@2 to 25.4 and 22.6, gains of 9.5 and 8.8 points. Under matched low-quality perturbations, average relative degradation is reduced from the strongest non-ours value of 14.6% to 11.1%. On Ego4D-NLQ and QVHighlights, EP-LVMQA remains broadly comparable to established task-specific localization methods.

cs.CVcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Multimodal agents use web and image tools to answer questions beyond their internal knowledge. Some search tasks use images mainly to identify an initial entity, then rely on text retrieval. These tasks offer limited insight into how agents connect information across images, objects, and search steps. We therefore focus on visual context management. This involves organizing and using visual information and linking it to other findings during search. Visually intensive tasks require agents to combine object appearance, local details, or spatial relations with external information. We introduce VisSearch-Bench, with 204 human-written tasks across 7 main domains and 25 subdomains. It covers Set-wise Visual Search, Sequential Visual Search, Hybrid Wide-Deep Search, Competitive Visual Disambiguation, and Visual-Structure-Guided Search. Each task provides a reference answer, traceable evidence, and scoring rules. Accuracy measures complete task success, while Process Score measures the correctness and completeness of individual results and subquestion answers. Across 16 multimodal models, the best system fully solves only 26.47% of tasks, even with search tools. These results highlight the challenge of linking visual evidence with search results to produce complete answers.

2026-11-03 13:27:22 | arXiv:2611.04874v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Optical Character Recognition (OCR) is among the oldest and most widely deployed applications of artificial intelligence. This survey reviews its development, focusing on the Foundation Model Era. We define OCR through faithful transcription: within a specified visual scope, OCR should completely reconstruct the text, notation, and document structure observable in the source image, and every textual, notational, or structural element in the output must be verifiable from visual evidence in that image. We trace the development from specialist OCR models toward unified models, examining joint text detection and recognition, structured sequence generation, and the use of pretrained vision-language models (VLMs). We organize contemporary OCR systems into five coexisting families: modular toolkits and task-specific models, specialized document VLMs, hybrid workflows, general-purpose VLMs, and commercial products and managed services. We compare their architectures, data, and training, examining hallucination and omission, insufficient spatial grounding, and inference cost in generative OCR. We review evaluation from character and scene text recognition to parsing individual structural elements and full-page transcription, highlighting gaps in consistent output definitions, attribution of performance to individual components, and coverage of real capture conditions and visible content and structure. Finally, we summarize the challenges facing OCR and discuss directions for future research. We continuously track related work at https://github.com/zhihengli-casia/Awesome-OCR-in-the-Foundation-Model-Era.

cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Latent diffusion and flow matching models are ubiquitous in state-of-the-art generative video models. While highly effective, relying on pretrained autoencoders to compress the data into a lower-dimensional latent space also comes with drawbacks. First, training proceeds in two stages: autoencoder pretraining, with compression and reconstruction objectives, followed by latent generative model training, precluding fully end-to-end training. Second, autoencoders also lead to an inherent loss of signal, and the trade-off between reconstruction quality and generative performance in the latent space is still an active area of research. In this paper we explore the viability of generative video models directly in pixel space. We analyze recent approaches in pixel-space generative modeling of images that rely on basic transformer designs, and identify several shortcomings that limit their effectiveness for the high-dimensional pixel-space video generation. To overcome these limitations, we propose a UNet-like transformer architecture along with improvements to the training recipe and sampling strategy. We conduct controlled experiments, training latent and pixel-space models from scratch on Kinetics-600. For the standard frames-to-video task at 128 resolution, our U-JiT model improves generative FVD by 27% over prior state-of-the-art results, and by 14% against the JiT pixel-space baseline. Scaling to higher 256 resolution, we find the improvement over pixel-space and latent-based models grows as we increase the spatiotemporal compression rate up to $64 \times 64 \times 8$. Finally, we find that U-JiT improves per-frame FID by 27% over latent models and 61% over pixel-space models on class-to-video generation, with respectively 9% and 56% reduction in FVD.

cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Fine-grained cross-view localization aims to precisely localize a ground camera against geo-referenced aerial imagery, but current methods typically assume a narrow geographic prior, substantially limiting their applicability when upstream localization remains coarse. To bridge this gap, we study this task over much larger aerial regions. However, this seemingly simple extension fundamentally changes the localization problem: the growing disparity in spatial extent between ground and aerial views makes reliable cross-view matching difficult to establish directly, while the increasing number of look-alike locations in larger regions introduces substantial visual ambiguity. To address these challenges, we propose RingMatch, a search-and-verification framework for wide-area cross-view localization. RingMatch first leverages predictive uncertainty to uncover plausible locations and progressively zooms into their surroundings to obtain increasingly fine-grained evidence, yielding a compact set of promising candidates. We then introduce the depth ring, a novel geometric representation that reveals subtle variations in the spatial layout around a location and serves as a view-shared spatial signature for an LVLM verifier to resolve residual ambiguity. To benchmark this setting, we construct VIGOR-Pro by composing VIGOR aerial tiles into contiguous regions with spatial extents of up to 400 m. Experiments show that RingMatch maintains strong localization performance as the aerial region expands, consistently outperforming representative end-to-end and retrieval-based methods.

MambaHand：基于Mamba的局部身份与行为序列学习用于动态手势认证

MambaHand: Local Identity and Behavioral Sequence Learning with Mamba for Dynamic Hand Gesture Authentication
Zenan Lin, Junduan Huang, Xilai Wang, Wenxiong Kang

2026-11-03 10:16:43 | arXiv:2611.04626v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Dynamic hand gesture authentication (DHGA) is an emerging biometric technique that contains both physiological and behavioral identity characteristics. However, existing methods primarily rely on convolutional networks or manually designed motion representations, making it difficult to jointly capture fine-grained local identity differences and long-range behavioral dependencies during hand gesture execution. To address these challenges, we propose a two-stream identity representation learning framework, MambaHand Net, to combine CNNs with Mamba for DHGA. The proposed framework consists of a Local Identity Stream (LIS) and a Behavioral Sequential Stream (BSS). The LIS extracts frame-wise local identity cues with a convolutional network, while the BSS preserves spatial tokens from intermediate visual features and constructs first-order and second-order temporal difference sequences to model multi-order behavioral variations. Moreover, the bidirectional Mamba is incorporated into the BSS to model semantic motion across different spatial locations, enabling efficient long-range modeling of hand gesture execution habits. Finally, attention-guided spatial token aggregation and a two-stream fusion strategy are used to integrate local identity cues and multi-order behavioral patterns into a discriminative identity representation. Our method achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) average performance on the SCUT-DHGA benchmark dataset, demonstrating that combining CNNs with Mamba is an effective new solution for DHGA.

Gradient-Guided Orientation Learning for Weakly Supervised Oriented Object Detection
Junyong Jang, Juheon Park, Minyoung Jeon, Jaehyup Lee
个性化推荐理由:

2026-11-03 10:07:49 | arXiv:2611.04616v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Weakly supervised oriented object detection (WS-OOD) aims to reduce the annotation cost of rotated bounding boxes by leveraging cheaper annotations such as horizontal boxes or points. However, these weak annotations provide no explicit orientation information, forcing existing methods to infer object angles through indirect geometric consistency or equivariant constraints. Such indirect supervision often becomes ambiguous and unstable under real-world appearance variations, including illumination changes, color shifts, shadows, and background clutter. In this paper, we present a simple yet effective gradient-guided orientation learning strategy for WS-OOD. Motivated by the observation that the input image itself contains useful orientation priors, our method extracts dominant gradient-based orientation cues from object regions and converts them into pseudo-angle labels for auxiliary supervision, enabling the detector to learn orientation-aware representations without requiring rotated bounding box annotations or extra learnable parameters. Furthermore, since AP can mask substantial angular errors when spatial IoU remains high, we introduce an orientation-aware evaluation metric that incorporates angular discrepancies into the standard AP protocol. Extensive experiments on weakly supervised oriented detection benchmarks demonstrate that the proposed pseudo-angle supervision improves both detection performance and orientation estimation quality, while the proposed metric provides a more faithful assessment of angle prediction than conventional AP alone.

SEEK-VAU：通过智能体搜索实现证据忠实的视频异常理解

SEEK-VAU: Towards Evidence-Faithful Video Anomaly Understanding via Agentic Search
Minghe Wang, Weijun Zeng, Dapeng Luo, Xinyue Wang, Nong Sang
个性化推荐理由:

2026-11-03 09:19:58 | arXiv:2611.04554v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Most video anomaly understanding (VAU) systems still infer anomaly judgments from evidence collected in a feed-forward way under predefined rules, typically sampled frames, or events clustered from such frames. This protocol fails when the collected evidence is incomplete under imperfect predefined rules, because once reasoning begins the model can no longer search for the missing evidence. We address this limitation with SEEK-VAU, which reframes VAU as an agentic search-and-verify interaction protocol under a bounded search budget: instead of passively reasoning over pre-collected evidence, the policy actively gathers evidence across the three stages of an anomaly event chain (precursor, trigger, and aftermath) and verifies its sufficiency before finalization, turning event-chain completeness into an explicit design rather than an outcome of predefined rules. A bounded budget caps the cost of this search, but does not by itself teach the policy where to look or when to stop. To make this behavior learnable, we introduce evidence-faithful counterfactual verification (EFCV), which rewards selected evidence that remains supportive, compact, and necessary under counterfactual verification. We further introduce SEEK-Bench, featuring video-level episodes with temporal interval annotations, semantic QA and event-chain stage labels. Together, SEEK-VAU and SEEK-Bench establish a strong foundation for evidence-faithful and actively verifiable VAU.

Electroencephalography (EEG)-based visual decoding and reconstruction aims to recover visual information from non-invasive brain signals. But there are challenges due to the complex temporal-frequency characteristics of EEG signals and the difficulty of discrete denoising framework in mapping neural activity to meaningful visual representations. Here, we propose E2I-Flow, an EEG-to-image decoding and reconstruction framework that combines a novel EEG encoder for time-frequency representation learning from EEG features, and conditional flow matching for visual decoding. A stationary-wavelet frequency branch and a temporal-mixing branch are integrated through bidirectional cross-attention and adaptive fusion, with the resulting EEG representations aligned to ground-truth image embeddings using contrastive and mean-squared-error loss supervisions. The aligned EEG representation is then used as a condition for a Transformer-based flow-matching model to predict the corresponding visual embedding for image synthesis. Experimental results show strong zero-shot EEG-to-image retrieval and improved visual reconstruction performance, outperforming several state-of-the-art baselines.

查看完整摘要
Video Reasoning Segmentation (VRS) aims to infer and segment target objects specified by reasoning queries in videos. Existing methods typically first infer the target and generate a few segmentation tokens for it, which are then fed into a mask decoder for mask prediction. However, such segmentation tokens tend to be both semantically monotonous and spatially ambiguous, making them largely insufficient to represent the target to guide mask prediction. In this paper, we propose an explicit visual object grounding framework for VRS. Specifically, we first propose a spatial-aware prompt generation and refinement scheme, in which we query a multimodal large language model to generate explicit spatial prompts (i.e., bounding box and point set) for the target and then refine them through multi-step dialogue. Furthermore, we introduce a query diversification and alignment module to generate auxiliary queries that describe the same target from different perspectives. We then enforce cross-query consistency between their predicted spatial prompts to alleviate the training bias caused by query limitations. Finally, we select several reliable frames using a prompt-based keyframe selection strategy to support mask decoding and propagation. Extensive experiments on eight datasets demonstrate that our method significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods. The code is available at https://github.com/viscom-tongji/EVOG.

Unified multimodal models (UMMs) integrate image generation and visual understanding, but are existing single-pathway unlearning methods sufficient to suppress target concepts across both? In this work, we study cross-pathway concept unlearning. Our goal is to prevent a model from both generating images of a target concept and identifying it in input images, while preserving other capabilities. To this end, we introduce UUBench, a diagnostic benchmark covering 30 target concepts and 60 semantically related neighboring concepts. Experiments on representative UMMs show that the evaluated single-pathway methods achieve limited joint forgetting: generation-side editors strongly suppress target generation but yield limited forgetting in visual understanding, while understanding-side baselines struggle to achieve substantial forgetting even in their own pathway. Their direct composition also yields limited joint forgetting. We therefore propose UniForget, achieving joint forgetting rates of 80.0% on BAGEL-7B-MoT and 92.7% on Janus-Pro-7B, outperforming representative baselines. Further analysis reveals trade-offs between joint forgetting and capability preservation.

Moving Object Segmentation (MOS) detects and separates dynamic objects from the static background in a video, which plays a fundamental role in dynamic-scene understanding. Most MOS methods rely on pure visual motion cues alone, such as optical flow or point tracks, which is inherently ambiguous due to the compounded camera and object motions as well as motion-induced appearance changes, e.g. shadows and reflections. Naive fusion of additional information such as depth and semantics, however, cannot leverage their complementarity to the fullest extent. We therefore propose DynOSeg, a novel MOS approach that takes on a two-stage fusion structure. Our experiments show that DynOSeg consistently outperforms published state-of-the-art MOS methods across a diverse range of datasets. Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) routinely name objects that are absent from the image. Latent steering can reduce this failure in one model forward pass, but a fixed gate treats early and late decoding steps by the same criterion. We observe that predictive entropy varies sharply within free-running generation, while states that commit to short answers occupy a narrow, confident band. We exploit this temporal mismatch by using uncertainty as a causally available, token-local control signal for an existing steering gate. We propose Entropy-Gated Dynamic Rectification (EGDR), which lowers the intervention threshold when entropy derived from the preceding step's already available logits is high and retains the calibrated operating point when it is low. On LLaVA-1.5-7B, the underlying static gate lowers CHAIR CS/CI from 53.00/15.30 to 32.00/14.30; replacing only its constant threshold with EGDR further lowers them to 29.00/13.60. The gain over the static gate holds on LLaVA-NeXT-7B and grows with the generation budget, while POPE scores and general multimodal capability remain effectively unchanged. EGDR requires no additional model forward pass and adds roughly 2% measured latency. Our code is available at https://github.com/alvi-uiu/EGDR.

cs.CVcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Visualizing multivariate scientific simulation data is essential for understanding complex physical phenomena, yet the massive scale of such data imposes a severe storage bottleneck in high-performance computing environments. Implicit neural representations (INRs) have emerged as a promising continuous alternative to traditional lossy compressors, but existing methods struggle to explicitly capture cross-variable interactions and to adapt to the diverse spectral characteristics of physical variables. To address these limitations, we propose SWAP-INR, an architecture for high-fidelity compression of multivariate, time-varying simulation data. SWAP-INR introduces two key components: (1) The switchable attention path (SWAP) module explicitly models cross-variable interactions through a cross-variable attention layer, then routes each variable to an attention or non-attention path by a deterministic, loss-driven criterion, and prunes the inactive layers for structural compression. (2) The adaptability amplification SIREN (A2-SIREN) replaces rigid frequency scaling with channel-wise learnable modulation, adapting to heterogeneous physical features with negligible overhead. Combined with post-training 16-bit quantization, SWAP-INR achieves state-of-the-art performance on small modular reactor (SMR) datasets and remains competitive on public physics simulation datasets, while maintaining a constrained memory footprint. Our code is https://github.com/hvcl/SWAP-INR.

For a decade, progress in artificial intelligence has been measured by how much a machine can do on its own. Large foundation models now write code and run multistep tasks, and personal agents act on a person's behalf. Yet people still forget what they once knew, miss what they are not looking at, fail to find the right words, and face hard choices without the benefit of their own history, because the machine that could help stands outside the situation. As AI moves into the physical world, attention has settled on one kind of intelligent individual, the embodied robot; we argue that the era of Physical AI holds a second kind of equal importance, the person augmented by AI. Because a symbiont changes what a person can do in front of others, it also raises risks of a new kind, from covert assistance in examinations to pressure on everyone to adopt it, and we treat their governance as part of the paradigm.

Medical physicists turn a prescription into a delivered dose and check one another's calculations, because one error in a beam calibration or a monitor-unit (MU) calculation can affect every patient treated with it. Reference answers come from code implementing the governing protocols, and a grader converts units and applies tolerance bands. From the same inputs, separate checking items test error detection, showing a colleague's worksheet or only the colleague's result, half correct and half with one planted error. Claude Opus 5.5 accepted 6 of 20 wrong results, although it applied the correction in all 20 of its own. We report the effect of two problems in our shielding items, found after the runs, and release the code, per-item outcomes and targeted-test responses.

2026-11-03 18:02:21 | arXiv:2611.05287v1 |
cs.CLcs.AIcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Fine-tuning a large language model (LLM) on harmful data in a narrow domain, such as bad medical advice, can make it broadly misaligned. How realistic this risk is remains unclear, since models are very rarely fine-tuned on explicitly harmful data in practice. In this work, we show that fine-tuning on data the model believes to be false but that has no harmful content, can also induce broad misalignment. Across mathematics, trivia, cooking and sports, the more strongly the model disbelieves the answers it is trained on, the more misaligned it becomes. In mathematics, misaligned answers rise from 4% to over 60% as the training answers become more wrong, with little loss of general capability. After fine-tuning, the model states the wrong answers but its multiple-choice probabilities still prefer the true ones, which suggests that misalignment comes from this internal inconsistency. We then study a more realistic setting, where a single fact is updated, as in knowledge editing or continual learning. The same relationship holds and the more the model still believes the true fact after training on the false one, the more misaligned it becomes. When we frame the same false fact as part of a coherent story, the model comes to believe it more deeply and becomes less misaligned than when we instruct it to assert the false fact. Content filters and persona vectors cannot tell these two framings apart, even though they lead to different levels of misalignment. These results suggest a risk for model updates, where training a model that does not adopt the new fact as true can induce misalignment even when the data looks harmless.

cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Financial question answering can fail even when a model extracts a plausible number from the supplied evidence: the answer may attach it to the wrong entity, period, unit, or accounting concept. Asking the model to verify and revise its draft can help, but open-ended revision may alter content that was already correct or inspect the wrong relation and reinforce the original error. We formulate repair as a decision that balances the information it may recover against the correct content it may damage. Based on this trade-off, we introduce Calibrated Answer Repair (CAR), which first estimates whether a draft is likely to benefit from repair, then selects a targeted check, localizes the relevant evidence or draft span, and limits the permitted edit. Across FinRank, Fin-RATE, and FinQA with three open-weight models, CAR outperforms generic verification in all dataset-model settings. At the system level, it recovers 14.6% of initially missed answer requirements while damaging 2.1% of initially correct ones. Its post-draft token cost is over 30 percent lower than the iterative revision alternatives. Ablations on two dataset-model settings show that evidence localization and bounded editing are the main contributors to CAR's gains.

这是谁的效果？目标如何夸大学习到的子空间干预

1/10
Whose Effect Is It? How Objectives Inflate Learned Subspace Interventions
Toygar Tanyel, Serkan Ayvaz
个性化推荐理由:

该标题讨论的是子空间干预的效果归因与目标函数膨胀问题，属于可解释性/因果推断方法论的范畴，未涉及LLM、Transformer架构、RecSys/Search/Ads或VLM类比等任何核心关注点。从标题看无明确应用潜力，与当前研究方向基本无关。

2026-11-03 15:06:46 | arXiv:2611.05033v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Interpretability often locates concepts by copying part of a language model's state from one input into another (activation patching). A larger output change counts as stronger evidence that this part carries the concept. Methods that learn which part to copy often change the output more than the whole state does. By that reading, learned parts isolate the concept more cleanly. Yet the whole contains the part. So whose effect is this excess, the network's or the method's? Copying the whole state learns nothing, so it shows what the network produces. We prove that, to first order, the effects of orthogonal parts add up to that of the whole. A part therefore exceeds the whole exactly when the rest moves the output back, which does not require isolating the concept more cleanly. Objectives that reward a larger effect select such parts. Distributed alignment search with cross-entropy, for example, exceeds the whole-state copy just before the output on almost all tasks, and inside the network at most sites. In both, changing only its objective to target what the network produces removes most of the excess. It therefore comes largely from the objective, so a larger learned effect alone is not stronger evidence.
