This study evaluates whether large language models (LLMs) can reliably replace or complement human research assistants in linguistically complex annotation. In a controlled annotation task, 73 human annotators (local students and crowdworkers with and without a background in linguistics) and more than 40 LLM configurations number-disambiguated 174 authentic uses of 'they' from Reddit, including innovative and context-sensitive cases. The best LLM, Kimi K3, reached 94.5% accuracy, above the human majority vote (86.2%) and the mean of the top half of human annotators (78.5%); only the single best human annotator scored higher (95.7%). LLMs also offer substantial practical advantages in cost and speed. However, qualitative error analysis reveals a crucial complementarity: human reasoning in low-agreement cases exposes inconsistencies in the annotation typology and thereby improves the methodological framework itself. The study contributes (i) a benchmark-style human-LLM comparison for a semantically demanding task, (ii) transparent cost and time evidence, and (iii) a methodological argument against a purely replacement-oriented view of automation. LLMs are viable for large-scale annotation support in linguistics, but their responsible use requires human critical oversight. The dataset with its gold-standard labels, all human and LLM annotations, and the fully reproducible code are openly available as a Quarto-based, public repository.

查看完整摘要
A privacy evaluation of de-identified text asks a language model (the adversary) to guess a person's protected attribute. We introduce a prior-normalised clipped leakage score of its confidence gain on the true value and audit the conclusions drawn from it. Each verdict depends on an evaluation interface (the queried person, answer options, prior, adversary model and scoring rule), which we vary on fixed releases of three corpora. In a stress test, an editor that writes the opposite-sex form at the same spans a redaction editor would redact misleads the adversary about most people, yet ranks first among editors scored on the same MedQA entries under this score, as under attack accuracy and proper scoring rules read as privacy. A relabelling-invariant reading of AUC ranks that editor sixth of eight, and a rule-aware adversary recovers the attribute from the same text. On ordinary releases most verdict changes are gains or losses of separability; of six choices varied over all pairs, only the adversary reverses comparisons (5 of its 124 changes), and the query can target the wrong person. SACC, a traceable editor we build as the instrument, lets us ablate two editing principles and show where its advantage does not hold.

查看完整摘要
Legal judgment prediction models may retain a guilty verdict after a legally decisive condition has been altered such that the specified charge is no longer established. We study this guilty bias via selective repair over matched charge-established and charge-not-established cases, where the unsupported verdict should change while its supported counterpart remains stable. We construct CAIL-MC, a legally reviewed dataset of 11,478 matched pairs covering 261 Chinese criminal charges. Held-out activation interventions show that contrasts learned from correctly resolved pairs transfer to false-guilty cases, but target-reaching interventions can also damage matched supported-guilty decisions. Motivated by this observation, we introduce PROSE, which reconstructs case-specific targets from a training-derived residual geometry, aligns them with a local margin-descent signal, attenuates supported-guilty-sensitive components, and realizes bounded targets through a preservation-regularized low-rank parameter update. Across Qwen3-8B and GLM4-9B, PROSE achieves stronger pairwise safe repair and evidence sensitivity, while preserving standard single-pass inference.

当真实标签并非真实标签：面向幻觉检测的自动标注

1/10
When Ground Truth Is Not Ground Truth: Automatic Labeling for Hallucination Detection
Rounak Sen, Divya Kumari, Deeksha Varshney
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于幻觉检测与自动标注，属于纯NLP-centric的评估与标签生成话题，明确属于不相关内容范畴。它与RecSys、Search、Ads的核心进展、Transformer架构或异构数据统一建模均无直接关联，故相关性极低。

2026-11-03 11:20:45 | arXiv:2611.04697v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Hallucinations in Large Language Models (LLMs) remain a major challenge, as they can produce misleading, factually incorrect, or unverifiable information, thereby undermining their trustworthiness and reliability. However, reliable ground-truth labels remain a key challenge in evaluating hallucination detectors. Recent studies often construct labels automatically by comparing model generations with reference answers using similarity metrics such as BLEURT. This implicitly treats BLEURT similarity as a proxy for hallucination, despite its English-centric supervision, unbounded score range, and design as a similarity metric rather than a factuality measure, making threshold-based ground-truth construction potentially unreliable. Through comprehensive experiments using 15 automatic supervision judges, seven hallucination detection baselines, two generator models, two QA datasets, and five languages, we demonstrate that detector performance varies substantially with the choice of supervision judge. Human validation reveals substantial differences in labeling reliability, with QGen-9B achieving the lowest overall misclassification rate (12.86%), while BLEURT and BLEURT-n exhibit rates of 47.23% and 47.32%, respectively. Our findings highlight the limitations of automatic similarity-based labeling and the need for more reliable judging methods that better capture semantic and factual correctness.

MIMIC is one of the most widely used public hospital datasets for machine learning on electronic health records, yet clinically important information, such as past medical history, allergies, and diagnostic findings, remains confined to free-text discharge summaries. Studies requiring this information must therefore build their own extraction pipelines, duplicating substantial effort across projects. 查看完整摘要
Prolonged exposure to hate speech on social media can cause lasting psychological distress among users. Counterspeech has emerged as an effective, non-censorial mitigation strategy, but its deployment requires rigorous quality assessment. Manual assessment is unscalable due to the large volume of content on the Web and imposes severe emotional strain on human annotators. Large Language Models (LLMs) offer a scalable alternative, yet concerns remain regarding their reliability. In this paper, we investigate the extent to which LLMs can effectively perform this task. We test 12 open-weight LLMs of different sizes and families under zero-shot, few-shot, and chain-of-thought prompting, to approximate pointwise and pairwise human expert assessment of counterspeech quality across four dimensions: relevance, coherence, aggressiveness, and suitableness. We find that (i) LLMs show low agreement and moderate rank correlations with human experts: our best configuration, Qwen 2.5 72B on coherence, achieves up to a linear-weighted Cohen's $κ_l = 0.19$ and Spearman's $ρ= 0.42$ in pointwise evaluation and an unweighted Cohen's $κ= 0.25$ in pairwise evaluation. (ii) Converting pointwise scores into pairwise labels substantially improves human--LLM agreement for selected configurations. For same-instance coherence, Qwen 2.5 72B under zero-shot prompting achieves $κ= 0.35$ with converted labels, compared with $κ= 0.25$ when judging pairs directly, and Mixtral 8$\times$7B under few-shot prompting improves from $κ= 0.07$ to $κ= 0.34$. (iii) LLMs agree more with one another than with human experts, particularly in pairwise evaluation, where the strongest model pair, OLMo 2 32B and Llama 3.3 70B on coherence, reaches substantial agreement with $κ= 0.76$, whereas their agreement with the experts in the same configuration does not exceed $κ= 0.21$.

Voluntary Servitude: Weaponized Safety Warning Mechanisms for Controlling AI Agents
Ilan Zendel, Matan Levy, Stav Cohen, Avi Mendelson, Amit LeVi
个性化推荐理由:

该论文关注AI智能体的安全警告机制及控制问题，属于安全与伦理范畴，与推荐系统、搜索或广告的核心技术发展无关。标题中未体现任何LLM架构、Transformer改进或推荐建模相关的技术贡献，因此与当前关注点极不相关。

cs.CRcs.AIcs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
LLM agents increasingly act on information retrieved from public sources they treat as credible, which turns a manipulated recommendation into a transaction, a dependency, or a decision with physical consequences. The security literature models this threat as instruction injection, in which an adversary writes a command into retrieved content and deployed defenses learn to recognize command syntax. We show that an attacker can instead steer an agent through the agent's own harm-avoidance. A single fabricated hazard, written as an ordinary review, leads the agent to disqualify the legitimate options and select the attacker's target by its own reasoning, with no command for a detector to recognize. We call this voluntary servitude. These payloads steer agents about as often as instruction injection (60.4% vs. 66.0%), and deployed injection detectors score them as benign, so behind a filter they are 1.7-4.4x more effective. We measure this at the generation stage across six models and ten consumer-service domains, for three attack families, and show that it transfers to production browser agents, outperforming instruction injection, and to a retrieval pipeline (1.0-57.0% end-to-end success, depending on the retriever). We then show that a corroboration requirement added to the agent's instructions removes roughly 95% of the exposure against a non-adaptive attacker without retraining. With the requirement in place, the agent still reads the planted hazard and no longer acts on it.

Do Multi-Turn Conversational Capabilities Carry Over from Text to Speech? A Parallel Multilingual Benchmark
Jiaming Zhou, Pei Zhang, Shiwan Zhao, Zhijun Wang, Baosong Yang, Yong Qin

2026-11-03 06:22:53 | arXiv:2611.04390v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Successful multi-turn voice interaction requires models to retain information from earlier turns, follow persistent instructions, and track revisions to conversational state. These abilities are usually evaluated from transcripts, leaving open whether successes demonstrated from text carry over when the same turns are spoken, and whether this transfer is consistent across languages. We introduce Omnilingua-AudioMC, a strictly parallel multilingual extension of Audio MultiChallenge, to study this question. The benchmark contains 354 conversations and 1,404 rubric items across ten languages, with content, turn structure, cross-turn dependencies, and evaluation criteria aligned across transcript and speech conditions. Across six speech-language models, the shift from reference transcripts to direct speech input is strongly model- and language-dependent: models with similar transcript performance can diverge substantially under speech, and their relative strengths across languages can change. Paired rubric-level analysis shows that aggregate transcript-speech gaps can obscure which conversational requirements are preserved, because losses of transcript-demonstrated successes can be offset by gains on other items. Replacing preceding user turns, the current turn, or both with model-generated transcripts localizes recoverable degradation: for the two models with the largest gaps, history substitution yields a larger macro-level improvement than current-turn substitution in all ten languages, while the strongest direct-speech model gains little from either. These results show that transcript performance is an unreliable proxy for multilingual spoken conversational ability and identify the representation of preceding spoken context as an important source of recoverable degradation. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/QwenLM/Omnilingua-Bench/tree/main/Omnilingua-AudioMC.

1/10
Neologism Inoculation Prompting
David Demitri Africa, Cameron Holmes
个性化推荐理由:

该标题指向一种针对LLM新词（neologism）鲁棒性的提示工程方法，属于纯NLP/LLM-centric的提示技术，未涉及搜索、推荐或广告中的排序、建模或Transformer架构创新。其潜在价值更多在于LLM本身的语言泛化能力，而非RecSys/Search/Ads的直接应用，因此相关性极低。

2026-11-03 03:52:15 | arXiv:2611.04260v1 |
cs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Inoculation prompting is a method to reduce undesired behaviours such as reward hacking in frontier models. It works by training a model on harmful examples under a prompt that specifies the undesired behaviour, which reduces the model's tendency to exhibit it unless prompted. However, the inoculation prompt is written in plain English, so any user can re-elicit the behaviour easily. We introduce neologism inoculation prompting (NIP), which replaces the English prompt with a new token that is trained on the frozen model and removed at deployment. NIP outperforms other inoculation methods, and performs competitively with gradient routing despite being simpler. It also works on reward hacking, but fails on sycophancy and harassment. Using control tokens and analyses of activations, we identify the mechanism for this as NIP hiding the harmful behaviour behind an unreachable region of model inputs, rather than deleting it. More broadly, our work suggests that neologisms can be a powerful way to carve out and shape access to behaviours that are undesired or harmful.

查看完整摘要
Understanding what kind of prompts users typically ask and how well an LLM performs on these prompt categories is crucial for LLM practitioners. The existing methods satisfy the demand by hierarchically clustering the user prompts and synthesizing the cluster names using an LLM to build a taxonomy. However, the cluster names from these methods are usually too general for humans to understand the detailed prompt distributions and identify the exact strengths and weaknesses of LLMs. In this study, we propose COMPREHEND, which automatically constructs an ultra-fine-grained entailment hierarchy for the prompts. The entailment hierarchy replaces the cluster names in the taxonomy with sentences to provide more precise cluster descriptions for more fine-grained categorization. Compared with EvalTree, a state-of-the-art taxonomy construction method, COMPREHEND improves an entailment/faithfulness metric from 22.6 to 41.7 and an informative metric from 16.3 to 39.1 in WildBench. Our experiments also show that the weakness report generated based on our entailment hierarchy is much more informative than EvalTree in terms of predicting the response scores in the benchmark. All the code is released in https://github.com/JackNapier20/COMPREHEND_public.

1/10
PACT: Certifying What Is Written in Parallel Decoding of Diffusion Language Models
Yanchao Li, Yusu Zhang, Jiaqing Xie, Yi Ban, Ben Gao, Zhongyu Huang, Tianfan Fu,...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文关注扩散语言模型的并行解码认证，属于纯NLP/语言模型解码理论与正确性验证范畴，与推荐系统、搜索或广告的核心技术（排序模型、特征建模、序列建模、Transformer效率改进等）无明显关联。其认证机制不涉及推荐/搜索/广告中的召回、排序或用户建模问题，也不属于可迁移的Transformer架构效率改进。

2026-11-03 01:41:41 | arXiv:2611.04152v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Parallel decoding speeds up masked diffusion language models by writing several tokens per forward pass. Published commit rules choose these tokens by testing the predicted distributions. Under sampling the written tokens are drawn, and these tests do not see them. We find that answer quality follows the reliability of the tokens actually written. An answer's log probability splits into this reliability and the dependence within each step. We propose PACT, a training-free commit rule that draws the tokens first, certifies the set it will write and spaces the positions of each step apart. This certificate holds at every temperature, and we show that no rule with the same guarantee commits more positions. Experiments use four models of the LLaDA and Dream families and HumanEval, MBPP, GSM8K, MATH500 and IFEval benchmarks. PACT reaches the quality of published commit rules with fewer forward passes. Spacing removes positions from each step, yet it usually lowers the total cost because later steps then write more tokens. At the same speed, published decoders also write a word twice in a row far more often than PACT. These results suggest that commit rules should certify the tokens they write. Code is available at https://github.com/LiZaiyuan0619/PACT.

2026-11-03 18:54:29 | arXiv:2611.05359v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
This paper presents GSCV++, a lightweight pipeline for 3D {G}aussian {S}platting (GS) sequences {C}ompression with standard {V}ideo codecs. To meet the requirements of low-complexity and hardware compatibility from the industry, GSCV++ jointly improves preprocessing, 3D-to-2D mapping, and practical video compression configuration of GS compression. First, for preprocessing, energy-adaptive inter-frame spherical harmonic (SH) alternative current (AC) dimensionality reduction (EA-SHDR) is proposed to produces a compact and stable temporal SH AC feature space. It can reduce the data volume of following mapping and practical compression, as well as less complexity while eliminating redundant information. Second, for 3D-to-2D mapping, prior-based Inter-MiniPLAS (P-MiniPLAS) is designed to generate smooth 2D feature maps through prior-based weighting factor inference and small scale remapping. Compared with vanilla Parallel Linear Assignment Sorting (PLAS) and Morton scan, P-MiniPLAS ensures the generated 2D maps are more suitable for the video codec while maintaining a low level of computational complexity. Third, for practical compression, content-aware generalized compression (CA-GC) is developed to use a non-uniform bit-depth split for coordinate map generation, and a coding unit size selection is introduced for efficient compression complexity control, which fully exploits the performance of video codecs without involving normative changes. Experiments demonstrate that GSCV++ reports over 20\% in intra- and 60\% in inter-prediction rate-distortion gains compared with state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods. GSCV++ also achieves faster 2D map generation, encoding, and decoding than other SOTA methods using the same type of video codecs. Ablation studies verify the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed modules and provide insights for subsequent research.

查看完整摘要
Automatic tools can now generate whole-brain MRI parcellations at cohort scale, but generated-label releases often provide little evidence about which cases are reliable enough to reuse. We introduce OpenBrain, a confidence-aware release of 35,838 provenance-verified OpenNeuro T1w cases from 607 source datasets. OpenBrain is organized as an auditable case-record release rather than a directory of standalone segmentation files: each record links a primary parcellation, two committee parcellations, source/license provenance, raw QC-7 measurements, percentile risks, a combined risk score R, and a confidence tier. QC-7 is a reference-free reliability panel over label geometry, image-label consistency, and committee disagreement, so users can filter, weight, audit, or re-threshold generated labels without rerunning inference. We evaluate this released evidence under a frozen protocol. Across external cohorts, lower QC-7 risk corresponds to higher generated-label quality; in fixed-budget training, lower-risk selections improve paired macro Dice over random selection by +3.2 to +3.7 percentage points. OpenBrain also exposes raw axes, committee outputs, provenance, and datasheet documentation, so confidence claims remain inspectable after download. It does not turn generated labels into manual annotations or clinical-grade segmentations. Its contribution is a large open generated-parcellation substrate, a reusable case-level risk-evidence layer, and an external validation protocol for release-time QC. Dataset: https://huggingface.co/datasets/lanqz7766/OpenBrain. Code: https://github.com/lanqz7766/openbrain.

However, classifying 3D point clouds becomes challenging in real-world scenarios where the density levels vary. These variations are due to limitations inherent to current sensing technologies and variable environmental conditions, which often prevent the capture of detailed and comprehensive geometric representations. Existing classification methods, which have demonstrated impressive results on dense point clouds, may be affected and could experience reduced performance when dealing with low-density point clouds. 2026-11-03 17:14:51 | arXiv:2611.05231v1 |

查看完整摘要
Deep models for radiological diagnosis are typically trained to predict disease labels directly from images, which limits them to diseases seen during training and makes their decisions hard to interpret in radiologists' terms. Radiology, however, proceeds from visual findings to disease through published guidelines that make this decision rule explicit, interpretable, and reproducible. We introduce diagnosis by composition (DbC), a representation learning framework that encodes this findings-first logic. DbC learns a vocabulary of radiological findings by aligning image features with the text embeddings of the findings, each finding represented as a dense map over the scan. A disease is defined by a set of required findings derived from its guidelines, and its diagnosis map is a fixed, parameter-free composition of the corresponding finding maps. Because the finding-to-disease mapping is derived from guidelines rather than learned, DbC is interpretable and auditable by design, can extend to new diseases by specifying their findings without retraining, and can diagnose unseen diseases by composing their findings. On multi-phase liver CT, DbC detects and localizes unseen diseases, rare diseases from a different institution, and an external cohort significantly better than all comparators, including models that align images with disease names. DbC also improves detection and localization in the fully supervised setting and remains superior across training-set sizes. Although demonstrated on liver CT, DbC applies wherever radiological guidelines define diseases through findings.

We introduce PLANTEUR, a benchmark for crop-type mapping from satellite images in the French overseas territories. Building on the PASTIS design, PLANTEUR provides georeferenced and aligned Sentinel-2, Sentinel-1, and Landsat-8 sequences with parcel-derived labels spanning six tropical regions and an 8-class label space. cs.CV
查看完整摘要
\textit{Objective:} Ultrasound videos are limited by hardware-constrained spatial resolution and signal-dependent speckle, while paired low- and high-resolution acquisitions for supervised restoration are rarely available. We aim to jointly super-resolve and despeckle ultrasound videos without paired training data or network retraining. \textit{Methods:} We propose UltraDiff, which uses a frozen pre-trained diffusion model (DM) as a plug-in prior and restores each frame by optimizing the latent seed of the DM sampler under a range-constrained maximum a posteriori formulation. We derive a speckle-aware data-fidelity term from a Rayleigh scale-family model and characterize its relative-residual and variance-adaptive weighting properties. We further introduce a stabilized fidelity that is bounded below and strictly convex in log intensity for positive observations in its unclamped form. A total-variation regularizer complements the DM prior, and a temporal seed update warm-starts each frame from the optimized seed of its predecessor. \textit{Results:} On EchoNet-LVH videos with simulated speckle and additive noise, UltraDiff with a natural-image prior improves PSNR by 1.24\,dB over the strongest baseline, and ultrasound-related priors improve it further. On real clinical echocardiography videos, UltraDiff reduces the mean absolute error of zero-shot ejection fraction (EF) prediction from 7.31 to 6.37 EF points relative to DUP, the strongest baseline. \textit{Conclusion:} Combining a speckle-aware fidelity with frozen diffusion priors enables ground-truth-free ultrasound video restoration that benefits downstream measurement. \textit{Significance:} UltraDiff needs neither paired data nor retraining and is not tied to a specific diffusion checkpoint. Code associated with this work will be made publicly available upon acceptance.

Tracking-by-propagation (TBP) methods perform end-to-end multi-object tracking by propagating track queries across frames, jointly handling detection and identity association without heuristic post-processing while naturally modeling temporal information. However, as detection and association are tightly coupled, detection queries in TBP are expected to discover currently untracked objects and provide complementary current-frame evidence for challenging tracks whose queries and identities are retained. Existing methods largely inherit detection-query formation from single-frame detection and construct such queries before the decoder with little awareness of trajectory states. Consequently, currently untracked objects may be missed, while challenging tracked objects with reduced visibility under occlusion may lack complementary evidence, causing association failures or duplicate responses. To address these issues, we propose TAQ-MOTR, a tracking-aware detection query formation framework that employs a pre-decoder Hybrid Query Generator (HQG) to fuse current-frame image features, track memory, and learnable memory. By conditioning detection queries on trajectory states, TAQ-MOTR adapts them to \rev{the discovery of currently untracked objects and tracked-object complementation We further introduce a training-only auxiliary detection branch whose one-to-one label assignment is decoupled from that of the main tracking branch, providing additional detection supervision to strengthen the shared HQG and decoder for the discovery of currently untracked objects. TAQ-MOTR improves performance on DanceTrack, SportsMOT, and BFT, achieving 72.0 HOTA, 63.1 AssA, and 76.6 IDF1 on the DanceTrack test set. Code is available at https://github.com/Magicmiya/TAQ-MOTR

Accurate vegetation-encroachment assessment and risk mitigation require electric utilities to accurately locate and characterize distribution-network assets, including poles and power lines, and distinguish among medium-voltage, low-voltage, neutral, and telecommunication lines. Mobile and aerial Light Detection and Ranging (LiDAR) point clouds provide detailed 3D representations of these assets and their surroundings. However, power-line detection and reconstruction remain challenging because conductor returns are often sparse, incomplete, noisy, or occluded by vegetation. These challenges are especially significant in distribution networks, where lower pole heights and complex line configurations place conductors close to trees. In addition, mobile and aerial LiDAR datasets differ substantially in point density, viewing geometry, spatial coverage, and occlusion patterns. We propose a robust and automated reconstruction method combining scaled density-based clustering, robust polynomial fitting, and iterative fragment merging to recover individual conductors under severe data incompleteness and occlusion. Following semantic classification, the same reconstruction pipeline processes both mobile and aerial LiDAR data without modality-specific code or parameter tuning. The method is evaluated through degradation experiments, benchmarking, parameter sensitivity analysis, and large-scale validation. On the aerial dataset, it achieves 96.87% precision, 96.66% recall, a 96.76% F$_1$-score, and a geometric RMSE of 0.127 m. On the mobile dataset, where conductor-level reference annotations were unavailable, it achieves a point-to-model RMSE of 0.045 m and a reconstruction coverage of 95.27%. Source code, documentation, and evaluation data are publicly available at https://github.com/varen-project.

Transfer learning from natural images is standard in supervised and increasingly adopted in self-supervised medical image analysis, but its role in generative modelling, and in particular diffusion denoisers, remains underexplored. Medical diffusion models typically train the denoiser from scratch, with no systematic evaluation of natural-image transfer. We analyse this across latent and pixel paradigms, model sizes (131M-952M) and two radiology datasets (RadImageNet, BraTS), and ask which aspects of transfer support generation. We find that: i) Natural-image initialisation consistently improves generation, by 21-34% relative gFID in pixel space against 14% in latent space, where the tokeniser supplies much of the prior. gFID gains mirror downstream pathology classification and radiomic-feature FD, both proxies for clinical fidelity. ii) Benefits reflect spatial structure, not semantics: semantic probing does not predict generation quality, whereas a spatial-structure metric adapted to internal denoiser representations tracks gFID at $|r|=0.85$, positioning spatial organisation as a channel through which pretraining manifests. iii) Mixed-domain denoiser training shows that natural and medical images share similar layer-wise representations up to the late blocks, and mixing natural images into medical training improves fidelity. iv) Transfer benefit is tied to natural image initialisation, outperforming in-domain medical pretraining and several reinitialisation schemes. Overall, natural image denoisers transfer positively to medical ones in our setup. Code is available at https://github.com/CAIROLab-Bern/rad-dit

cs.CVcs.RO
查看完整摘要
We propose PadLoc, a training-free localization back-end that places a moving sensor within a known floor plan. Floor plans are cheap, persistent, and robust to appearance change, but recent learned-depth methods localize against them by filtering per-frame depth through dense $(x,y,θ)$ pose grids. PadLoc keeps the swappable depth front-end and replaces the dense grid back-end with sparse geometric matching: dominant wall orientations define a small pose-candidate set, masked normalized cross-correlation scores observed free space against the plan, and a moving-pose Gaussian-mixture Bayes filter plus consensus readout decodes the trajectory. Holding each competitor's depth fixed, PadLoc matches or beats UnLoc's forward filter and the F3Loc filter at every Gibson trajectory length, and UnLoc's offline refinement from $T=35$ on; it exceeds Z-FLoc's published results with a shared MapAnything front-end. On Bonn LiLocBench, PadLoc improves pooled SR@1m over UnLoc by 23-32 pp under wheel odometry and 35-43 pp with ground-truth motion; with full-FOV LiDAR plus wheel odometry the same readout reaches 100% SR@1m at 9-10 cm median error on the static sequence. On the Hilti-Trimble Localization Challenge PadLoc localizes a camera-only fisheye rig on construction floors hundreds of metres across at 0.90-0.98 online SR@2m on three of five sessions; adding learned wall isolation, applied to every session, covers all five at 0.67-0.92 with the localizer unchanged throughout. PadLoc thus matches or outperforms dense grid back-ends while preserving sensor modularity and a compact state that does not grow with trajectory length.

Public cervical cytology benchmarks release many cell crops cut from far fewer microscope fields or slides and are often split at the crop level, so test cells share acquisition parents with training cells. In a preregistered study, crop-level splitting raised SIPaKMeD accuracy by 0.97 percentage points across twelve architectures and twenty seeds (95% CI 0.59-1.39), and CRIC accuracy by 12.08 points (11.16-12.99). On SIPaKMeD, a factorial that fixes training crop and parent counts attributes the gain to sibling exposure (1.73 points, 0.95-2.51), not to the extra parent diversity of crop-level splits (0.00). Parent identity is decodable at about 100 times chance, yet erasing 82% of its above-floor linear recoverability left the gap unchanged, and Grad-CAM did not distinguish leaking from clean models; matching test cells' color statistics to an unseen same-class field removed most of the advantage. Decodability and saliency cannot substitute for a parent-disjoint split.

查看完整摘要
High-fidelity photovoltaic (PV) module mapping requires complete instance recovery and accurate delineation. Vision foundation models offer zero-shot segmentation, but converting incomplete and inconsistent predictions across views into a complete, geometrically accurate map of individual modules remains challenging. We propose the Multi-View PV Module Mapper (MV-PVMapper), a training-free framework coupling vision foundation models with unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) photogrammetry. First, Segment Anything Model 3 (SAM 3) generates candidates from original images using text prompts. Their contours are then transferred into object space through per-module slope correction to limit distortion from surface-model noise, and verified against representative module dimensions to guide cross-view instance selection. Finally, accepted instances return to all covering images as box prompts for SAM~3, supplementing text prompts with spatial cues for iterative segmentation, verification and fusion. On five-direction surveys of three operational rooftop PV plants at approximately 1 cm nadir ground sampling distance, two feedback iterations recover 7,710 of 7,711 reference modules with no false positives at an intersection-over-union (IoU) matching threshold of 0.5. Macro-averaged area precision and recall reach 99.00 % and 98.52 %, respectively. Averaged across sites, the aggregated Jaccard index reaches 97.55 %, compared with 60.73 % and 41.24 % for orthomosaic segmentation and multi-view pixel-voting baselines, respectively; average precision at the stricter IoU threshold of 0.95 reaches 95.67 %. Ablation studies show that module-size constraints are critical for instance separation, while slope correction and feedback to all covering views improve boundary accuracy. These results are achieved without task-specific image annotation or segmentation-model weight updates.

Accurate segmentation of the mitral valve from three-dimensional transesophageal echocardiography (3D TEE) is severely restricted by the scarcity of publicly available expert- annotated volumetric datasets. To address this critical limitation, we investigated Fourier Domain Adaptation (FDA), utilizing general-purpose generative-AI images (from platforms like Chat- GPT, Google Gemini, and Microsoft MAI) as annotation-free style donors for 3D mitral valve segmentation. We employed a DeepMitral residual U-Net model on the public MVSeg2023 dataset, comparing six conditions over three runs: real-only training, traditional augmentation, FDA with each generative source, and a non-AI FDA control using real-image style donors, plus an optimization-matched real-only control and a \b{eta} sensitivity analysis. Case-level paired testing showed that FDA with these generative sources led to significant reductions in boundary- and surface-related errors across all three donor conditions relative to the primary real-only baseline. However, the impact on the core segmentation metric, Generalized Dice Score (GDS), proved generator-dependent. ChatGPT-FDA showed a significant GDS advantage over the optimization-matched control but not over the primary baseline, and Gemini-FDA showed no significant GDS difference. Notably, only MAI-FDA provided a statistically robust and significant increase in GDS compared to the primary baseline. The \b{eta} sensitivity was not robust across training runs. These findings confirm that FDA remains an important augmentation mechanism. While generative-AI images offer a practical means of acquiring style diversity without new annotations, the non-AI control performed comparably to ChatGPT-FDA, so we do not conclude that this approach is superior to training with real-image donors.

查看完整摘要
Vertical forest structure influences fire behavior by regulating understory-to-canopy fuel connectivity, yet operational fuel-type models represent this complexity through discrete classes that only indirectly capture vertical continuity. High-density point clouds offer an opportunity to characterize vertical fuel connectivity in a more explicit and continuous manner, but specific methodological approaches are still required. We applied Gaussian decomposition to vertical height profiles derived from terrestrial laser scanning (TLS) point clouds across 25 permanent forest plots to characterize geometric vertical fuel continuity. A multiscale analysis spanning from the plot scale to ~0.5 $m^2$ units was used to evaluate geometric, probabilistic, and overlap metrics for characterizing fuel strata. The method reproduced the vertical transition between shrub and tree strata, with median Pearson correlations ranging from 0.83 to 0.96 across analysis levels. Probability density overlap metrics and separability indicators, such as the Jeffries-Matusita distance, provided complementary descriptors of vertical continuity. Intermediate spatial scales (3-6 m cells) provided a useful compromise between spatial detail and the consistent representation of shrub-canopy interaction patterns. Finer scales increased variability due to point density reduction and profile irregularity, and low mean squared error values were found insufficient as sole performance indicators. Gaussian decomposition applied to TLS point clouds enables explicit quantification of vertical fuel continuity, a limitation of current discrete classification systems. Continuous vertical connectivity metrics derived from this approach offer a pathway for improving ladder fuel characterization and strengthening decision-making support in wildfire risk assessment.

2026-11-03 15:43:03 | arXiv:2611.05084v1 |
cs.CVcs.RO
查看完整摘要
Repeated robotic monitoring requires localization across visits to the same environment, even if the environment changes. Such changes can substantially reduce persistent visual content, and repetitive structures can lead to incorrect cross-session correspondences. In this paper, we address visual multi-session registration of a new data recording (the query session) to a previously reconstructed reference map. Under substantial visual and structural changes, cross-session localization may be possible only for parts of the trajectory. In our approach, we therefore rely on a sparse set of reliable absolute pose constraints and combine them with within-session relative-pose information to register the complete trajectory to the reference map. We first estimate absolute alignment hypotheses through visual place recognition, local feature matching, and geometric verification. We then use sequence consensus to reject hypotheses that are inconsistent with a common query-to-reference alignment and retain the remaining poses as absolute anchors. These anchors are refined through featuremetric alignment against a Gaussian feature field and combined with within-session relative-pose constraints in a robust pose-graph optimization. We evaluate our approach on 15 cross-session experiments from the challenging HILTI26 construction-site dataset, covering substantial scene changes and difficult-to-match environments. Our method achieves 94.4% recall within 1m and 100.0% within 5m, outperforming independent image localization, which peaks at 65.3% and 70.7%, respectively, as well as direct registration of independently reconstructed Structure-from-Motion geometry. Our results show that sparse, reliable absolute anchors can provide accurate and complete trajectory registration even when reliable cross-session correspondences are available only for part of the query session.

2026-11-03 15:36:04 | arXiv:2611.05072v1 |
cs.ROcs.AIcs.CV
查看完整摘要
Although existing candidate trajectory evaluators have substantially improved end-to-end planning accuracy, their underlying mechanism remains suboptimal. Model-based evaluators typically make scoring future-aware by explicitly predicting candidate-conditioned future states over the planning horizon, which we term rollout. However, rollout incurs high inference cost and accumulates prediction errors. We argue that planning does not require reconstructing a full future scene for each candidate, but only future-relevant information for reliable comparison. We therefore propose RFWorld, a Rollout-Free World model for end-to-end trajectory evaluation. RFWorld constructs a shared scene memory and shapes it into a future-readable representation via lightweight training-time temporal readout with semantic supervision, keeping training overhead low. At inference time, candidate trajectories directly query the shared memory via sparse spatial readout for scoring, reducing computation by roughly the planning horizon times the number of candidate trajectories, while avoiding recursive rollout errors. Experiments on NAVSIM and Bench2Drive show that RFWorld learns a more planning-aligned world representation and achieves state-of-the-art performance.

查看完整摘要
Pretrained video generation models distill internet-scale motion priors into robot policies, but full video is a redundant signal for action prediction. We argue that dense 3D motion is the medium through which video pretraining best transfers to robot actions, and that predicting motion rather than video affords better sample-efficiency for action prediction. FloMo is a 5B video-generation model fine-tuned to predict 3D scene-flow and action tokens. Rendering scene flow as RGB and encoding it into the same latent space as video enables a pretrained video generation backbone to efficiently adapt to predict 3D scene flow. Co-trained on action-free human video and action-labeled robot teleoperation data, our model benefits from human-video pretraining and generalizes to unseen objects, motion primitives, and tasks. Replacing motion prediction with a video prediction objective degrades out-of-distribution generalization, while adding a video prediction target alongside motion lowers policy success rather than improving it. When motion, video, and action are predicted jointly, action tokens attend far more to motion than to video, suggesting that predicting video wastes model capacity. Together, these results indicate that motion effectively mediates the transfer from video pretraining to action. More information can be found at flomo-wam.github.io.

Footstep patterns, captured using pressure or force-sensitive walkways during natural walking, offer a convenient and passive approach to biometric recognition. However, footwear variability poses a major challenge to practical deployment, as it impacts both the foot-sensor interface and the user's underlying gait mechanics. This study considers the particularly difficult scenario where users enroll under one fixed footwear condition but must later be recognized in arbitrary, unseen footwear. Using the StepUP-P150 dataset, we show that footwear-induced shift, while substantial and heterogeneous across individuals, contains shared structure that can be learned from a training population and transferred, zero-shot, to previously unseen users and footwear styles. We do this using BiStepNet, a novel architecture that decomposes spatial and temporal identity cues into independently trainable streams and outperforms compact 3D spatiotemporal baselines for cross-footwear recognition. We demonstrate that cross-footwear recognition performance scales systematically with the volume and diversity of footwear representation in the training set, with gains transferring to footwear styles not directly observed during training. Moreover, we show that auxiliary gait datasets with only partial modality overlap (incorporated through the architecture's decomposed design) can be leveraged to further improve robustness to unseen footwear conditions.

cs.LGcs.CV
查看完整摘要
Deep learning in medical imaging is constrained by data scarcity and by privacy regulations that make centralizing patient data across institutions impractical. Federated Learning (FL) addresses these constraints by keeping data local, but its distributed nature exposes training to strong statistical heterogeneity. Multicentric medical data is particularly affected, exhibiting a mixed skew that combines feature, label, concept, and quantity shifts across centers. Personalized FL mitigates heterogeneity by tailoring a model to each client, yet most methods assume training from scratch, which is unrealistic given the high dimensionality of medical features relative to the limited data available. We propose the Personalized Aligner Federated Pretrained (PAFP) framework, a personalized FL approach built on a pre-trained backbone. PAFP inserts client-specific Feature Alignment modules before the fine-tuned blocks to realign hidden feature distributions toward a server-aggregated target. On the FedISIC benchmark, PAFP attains the highest unweighted balanced accuracy while reducing inter-client performance variance roughly six-fold relative to the strongest baseline, yielding accuracy and cross-client equity jointly. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/Alexandre-Leclercq/PAFP.

Saliency maps are offered as a safeguard for medical image classifiers, but whether they reveal individual errors at a new hospital remains largely untested. We trained a convolutional network and a vision transformer on NIH ChestX-ray14 and evaluated 303,768 decisions per model at the training hospital and at two external sites, in the USA and Vietnam, with radiologist-consensus labels. We scored each explanation by its anatomical enrichment, the area-normalized concentration of attribution in a segmented thoracic region, and tested whether it detects errors beyond the model's confidence. 查看完整摘要
Requested-value control is stricter than continuous steering: given $c$ on a human-rating scale, the generated image should attain that value from only limited ratings. We present ScoreDial, which fits a differentiable regressor $R$ to 100-479 human-rated images; once $R$ is fixed, a requested value $c$ is defined on its output scale, and learned target conditioning is paired with error-adaptive guidance that repeatedly reduces $|R(\mathbf{x})-c|$ during sampling. Across tire slip resistance, interior visual complexity, food desire-to-eat, and soap translucency, using one set of sampler settings shared across all four, ScoreDial reaches requested values and achieves, on all four attributes, $R$-MAE at most 0.013 over a range of width 0.8 and Spearman $ρ=0.980$, the maximum attainable under our evaluation protocol. Because $R$ also drives sampling, we evaluate agreement with $R$ separately from matched sweeps, an independent auditor fitted to the same ratings on a backbone none of the deployed regressors uses, and blinded human judgments. MPGD-flow also reaches the requested values under $R$, but ScoreDial has larger auditor displacement on every attribute, the largest among all compared methods, and a stronger MPGD-flow setting gives up target agreement without closing this gap. On every attribute, removing target conditioning leaves low $R$-MAE but more than halves auditor displacement, separating precise score correction from attribute movement. Blinded rating studies with 15 raters per attribute show that mean human ratings track $c$ on tire and soap, with linear $R^2=0.665$ and $R^2=0.786$, and in forced-choice comparisons of matched sweeps, raters chose ScoreDial's as the clearer progression in at least 84% of judgments against every baseline.

Gait biometrics aims at identity recognition by appearance or pose features captured during unconstrained human walking. Existing methods predominantly rely on binary silhouette images, inherently characterized by low information entropy, and fail to fully utilize the dynamic information in gait sequences, limiting their ability to handle cross-time variations effectively. Consequently, these methods encounter significant challenges in the wild. They are susceptible to variations in clothing, carried objects, and viewpoints, as well as dynamic environmental conditions, all of which lead to substantial degradation in recognition accuracy and robustness. To overcome these challenges, we propose a pose-knowledge-aware approach specifically designed for robust gait recognition in the wild. First, we propose a novel Dynamic Pose-Parsing enhanced Silhouette gait representation paradigm that substantially enhances the information entropy and effectively captures fine-grained behavioral cues by integrating pose-parsing cues with traditional silhouettes. Subsequently, we develop a dual-branch Dynamic Pose-Knowledge-Aware Gait network. The framework consists of two complementary branches: (1) a 2D dynamic pose-aware branch that extracts pose-enhanced features from our proposed DPPS representations, and (2) a 3D Skinned Multi-Person Linear branch that leverages the 3D SMPL model to capture rich and comprehensive 3D dynamic pose information. Finally, the features are integrated to facilitate accurate and robust gait recognition. Extensive experiments on four challenging in-the-wild gait datasets demonstrate the effectiveness and generalizability of the proposed DPPS representation. The complete framework is further evaluated on SUSTech1K, CCPG, and Gait3D, where SMPL information is available or can be consistently estimated, achieving competitive performance under diverse real-world conditions.

Yiwen Dou, Yiqiang Ding
个性化推荐理由:

2026-11-03 14:08:33 | arXiv:2611.04940v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
High-order dependencies among views can be modeled by tensor singular value decomposition (t-SVD) in multi-view subspace clustering. However, the tensor nuclear norm often used with this framework assigns the same asymptotic marginal penalty to singular values of different magnitudes, which may excessively shrink dominant components that encode cluster structure. We propose HTNN-MSC, which replaces this norm with a bounded hyperbolic tangent tensor rank surrogate. The surrogate is strictly concave on the positive half-axis and has a Lipschitz continuous derivative. Its marginal penalty decreases as singular values grow. We develop an alternating direction method of multipliers solver in which the tensor subproblem decomposes into scalar frequency-domain shrinkage problems. Safeguarded multi-start Newton iterations and candidate-objective comparison implement the resulting hyperbolic tangent thresholding operator. The shape parameter is scaled by the input spectrum. We also establish o-minimal definability of the augmented objective. Under explicit boundedness, descent, relative-error, and exact-block conditions, the Kurdyka-Lojasiewicz framework guarantees convergence of the full sequence to a KKT point. On 23 datasets, HTNN-MSC achieves a mean clustering accuracy of 0.8404 and an average accuracy rank of 2.78 against nine representative methods. Friedman tests and Holm-adjusted Wilcoxon comparisons reveal significant overall differences, although several pairwise differences remain nonsignificant. HTNN-MSC records 19 wins, one tie, and three losses against the convex t-SVD-MSC skeleton. A same-skeleton ablation also shows that the proposed surrogate does not uniformly outperform alternative nonconvex penalties.

2026-11-03 14:07:46 | arXiv:2611.04939v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Robustness verification is essential for ensuring the safety and security of neural networks, but current methods focus heavily on norm-ball attacks, which do not adequately model typical real-world threats that present physical perturbations, known as patch attacks. A naive approach requires computationally prohibitive enumeration of all possible patch positions. To address the challenge, we propose a novel neural network verification method specialized to the patch-attack threat. Instead of enumerating patch locations, we first model the patch attack with group-wise sparsity and formulate its verification methods as a mixed-integer semidefinite program (MISDP) by applying SDP relaxation to the neural network. We then propose three relaxations, including Group-Lasso relaxation, that remove the integer variables while preserving the sparse, group-wise structure of the original attack, enabling substantially efficient verification. Our empirical analysis demonstrates that the proposed relaxations require less than 3% of the computation time of the original MISDP, while the Group-Lasso relaxation incurs only a 0.34\% average increase in the objective value over the original formulation. Furthermore, Group-Lasso achieves a verified rate of 91%, compared with under 70% for prior methods.

MiCaDe: Online LiDAR-Camera Miscalibration Change Detection for Autonomous Driving
Carlo Sgaravatti, Riccardo Pieroni, Matteo Corno, Sergio M. Savaresi, Luca Magri...

2026-11-03 13:52:45 | arXiv:2611.04912v1 |
cs.CVcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Reliable multi-sensor perception in autonomous vehicles requires accurate calibration between cameras and LiDAR. However, calibration drift is often unavoidable in long-term deployments: vibrations, thermal effects, and minor mechanical shocks may alter the relative pose between sensors, leading to systematic errors in downstream fusion tasks. We propose MiCaDe (Miscalibration Change Detection), an online solution detecting when the extrinsic LiDAR-camera calibration parameters are no longer accurate by means of sequential change detection. MiCaDe requires no calibration targets, or modification of the perception stack, providing low false-alarm rates with negligible computational overhead. Our key insight is that 3D and 2D bounding boxes produced by standard object detectors should remain geometrically consistent under correct calibration. By matching cross-modal detections, we derive geometric consistency metrics that serve as sensitive indicators of extrinsic drift. These metrics are aggregated into fixed-size batches and scored against a reference distribution, producing a likelihood stream that is monitored by a Change Point Model (CPM). Crucially, the CPM accumulates statistical evidence over time, enabling detection of subtle drifts that would otherwise go unnoticed. Experiments on multiple long-sequence datasets and on a real-world dataset with physically induced miscalibration confirm reliable detection under diverse conditions. Code available at: https://github.com/CarloSgaravatti/MiCaDe.

cs.ROcs.CV
查看完整摘要
Video world models enable candidate actions to be evaluated through imagined outcomes, but high predicted rewards can favor trajectories with poor execution quality. We introduce Trusting Imagined Futures (TIF), an offline supervision selection method for vision-language-action (VLA) post-training that audits predicted rewards using execution feedback. A multidimensional reward model predicts a utility score for candidate ranking and five dimensions of execution quality. An auditor uses reward predictions and uncertainty to identify overestimation patterns learned from paired imagined videos and real executions. TIF accepts the candidate with the highest predicted utility when its advantage over the existing trajectory exceeds a margin and its audit risk passes a threshold; otherwise, it retains the existing supervision. The retained real trajectories then supervise behavior cloning. Across four LIBERO suites and three training seeds, TIF achieves a mean success rate of 76.25%, improving over training on the existing trajectories by 3.00 percentage points with the same trajectory count and training budget. Adding audit gating yields 1.47 percentage points with the reward model and advantage margin held fixed. The gains persist against advantage ranking and logistic regression risk selection at matched replacement counts. On recorded videos from three real robot tasks, TIF increases clean completion among selected trajectories from 58.33% to 70.00% across 60 candidate pools.

Nighttime images are inherently susceptible to complex, compound degradations, making accurate quality assessment exceptionally challenging. Score-based IQA methods condense quality into a single number, lacking the multi-dimensional, language-grounded reasoning required for nighttime evaluation. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) offer a more comprehensive alternative, yet remain constrained by a critical data gap: existing real-world IQA datasets are overwhelmingly daytime-centric and often built from outdated captures, while synthetic alternatives lack nighttime-specific degradation modeling, yielding artifacts far removed from authentic nighttime conditions. To bridge this gap, we present NightIQA, a dataset and benchmark suite purpose-built for MLLM-based nighttime IQA. It comprises three core components: NightIQA-Real, a real-world dataset of 1,769 high-resolution images that we captured ourselves with modern equipment, encompassing six canonical distortion types and structured multi-dimensional annotations; NightIQA-Bench, a benchmark of 1,078 visually grounded multiple-choice questions tailored to MLLM-based nighttime IQA, organized around a four-level evaluation hierarchy spanning Perception, Technical Assessment, Localization, and Reasoning; and NightIQA-Syn, an inherently scalable, scene-aware synthesis pipeline that jointly models global-level and instance-level degradations, with an initial release of 80k high-fidelity images alongside the full generation code. Fine-tuned on NightIQA with Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), Qwen3-VL-8B outperforms open-source IQA methods and achieves higher overall accuracy than leading closed-source models, highlighting the value of domain-specific data and structured supervision.

LIPS-PC：用于单像素分类的光照模式学习

1/10
LIPS-PC: Learning Illumination Patterns for Single-Pixel Classification
Tomer Porat, Talya Eden, Eliahu Cohen
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于单像素成像与计算摄影中的光照模式学习，属于视觉/成像硬件领域的特定方法，与推荐系统、搜索或广告的核心问题无关。其技术不涉及LLM、Transformer架构或面向用户序列/特征的统一建模，因此与当前关注方向基本无交集。

2026-11-03 12:18:45 | arXiv:2611.04780v1 |
physics.opticscs.CVcs.LG
查看完整摘要
In single-pixel (ghost) imaging, known illumination patterns probe an object and a detector with no spatial resolution returns one number per pattern. Each measurement costs acquisition time and photons and, for ionizing probes, contributes to delivered dose. Motivated by automated radiographic defect screening, where a class label suffices, we introduce Learning Illumination Patterns for Single-Pixel Classification (LIPS-PC). The patterns are learned end-to-end while the measurement operator remains one fixed linear matrix, identical for every input. We parameterize it as a residual correction to a two-dimensional discrete cosine transform (2D-DCT), $D=D_{\mathrm{base}}+Δ$, and jointly train the correction and a compact classifier using cross-entropy, without a reconstruction objective. This yields a task-driven linear embedding through optimization. We evaluate LIPS-PC numerically on MNIST and Fashion-MNIST under a controlled protocol, then on RIAWELC, a real industrial X-ray weld-radiograph dataset. Across five budgets, $M\in{8,16,32,64,128}$, corresponding to sampling ratios from below 1% to 12.5%, the learned operator outperforms the best tested fixed transform by 9-15 percentage points at $M=8$ on all three datasets. Its eight-measurement accuracy requires roughly 16-32 measurements with the best fixed transform, indicating a $2\times$ to $4\times$ saving at comparable accuracy. The advantage generally shrinks as $M$ increases, with a small reversal on RIAWELC at higher budgets. These results show that learning what to measure is especially valuable when measurements are scarce.

We introduce iMED, the first synchronized multi-endoscope dataset for robot-assisted minimally invasive surgery (RAMIS), designed to address a fundamental limitation in surgical vision benchmarking: the absence of independent held-out viewpoints. Existing surgical datasets capture single-trajectory sequences from a single endoscope, making it impossible to distinguish geometric generalization from photometric interpolation along a narrow forward-facing path. iMED provides 340 sequences ~170K synchronized timepoints with 4 views per timepoint) recorded simultaneously from two independent stereo endoscopes across ex vivo, postmortem, and live surgical settings, spanning 14 specimens with diverse anatomical regions and motion regimes. The dataset includes calibrated camera intrinsics, ArUco-based frame-wise pose estimates with uncertainty quantification, instrument segmentation masks, and rich clinical metadata. Using a train-on-one-endoscope, test-on-another evaluation protocol, we benchmark 23 state-of-the-art methods across rigid and deformable novel view synthesis, pose estimation, feature matching, and monocular depth estimation. Our experiments show that methods relying primarily on photometric supervision degrade substantially under this held-out-endoscope setting, while methods with explicit geometric regularization are more robust across views in our benchmark. Notably, large-scale foundation models transfer surprisingly well to surgical imagery, often outperforming domain-specific models, suggesting pitfalls in current fine-tuning protocols. iMED establishes a new evaluation protocol for geometric generalization in surgical vision.

有界AUKS：一种通过有界Kolmogorov-Smirnov曲线下面积进行异常检测的新型评估指标

1/10
Bounded AUKS: A Novel Evaluation Metric for Anomaly Detection via Bounded Area Under the Kolmogorov--Smirnov Curve
Yanshuo Zhang, Juan Du
个性化推荐理由:

该论文提出了一种用于异常检测的新评估指标，属于纯评估/理论范畴，与推荐系统、搜索或广告的核心技术（如模型架构、排序算法、LLM应用）无直接关联。评估指标类工作不属于我关注的方向，且未涉及任何可迁移至RecSys/Search/Ads的使能技术。

2026-11-03 12:06:45 | arXiv:2611.04754v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Recent literature widely used global metrics such as AUROC and AUPRO to quantify the detection performance. AUROC and AUPRO scores are approaching 100% in some public datasets such as MVTec AD dataset and VisA dataset, which misleadingly suggests that the pixel-level anomaly detection task has already been solved. Threshold-independent metrics represented by AUROC inherently ignore the threshold selection process, which is inevitable in practical applications. Although AUPRO restricts the False Positive rates (FPRs) to an acceptable range, extremely low FPRs can lead to increased false negative rates (FNRs). We argue that there is a lack of metrics capable of quantifying the separation between the anomaly score distributions of normal and anomalous samples, which hinders advancements in anomaly detection. In response, we propose the Bounded Area Under the Kolmogorov--Smirnov curve (Bounded AUKS), a partial integral metric that integrates the difference between TPR and FPR within a data-driven bounded range. Specifically, we introduce AUKS from KS integral as a direct measure of separability between the anomaly score distributions. The bounded range is determined only by score distributions of normal test samples. Lower bound excludes the uninformative high-FPR region. Upper bound avoids evaluation in extreme regions that aggressively push FPR towards zero, which result in a very low recall. Our experiments on 9 models across MVTec AD and VisA datasets show that Bounded AUKS can address the limitations of existing metrics and has a better performance in evaluating unsupervised anomaly detection models.

The reconstruction of fragmented historical textiles presents a significant challenge in computational archaeology. The Oseberg ship burial, dating to 834 CE, yielded hundreds of degraded textile fragments that have resisted systematic computational analysis. While deep learning has revolutionized image-based puzzle solving, progress on archaeological textiles has been limited by the lack of domain-specific datasets and systematic evaluation of modern architectures. To address this gap, we present TEXMET, a curated dataset of 18,644 historical textile images from the Metropolitan Museum of Art's Open Access collection, representing the first textile-specific benchmark for puzzle-solving research. Using TEXMET, we conduct a systematic evaluation of three state-of-the-art puzzle-solving models: JPDVT, FCViT, and DiffAssemble. Our experiments reveal that the transformer-based models pre-trained on standard datasets fail to generalize to textiles, but domain-specific fine-tuning proves highly effective. The graph-based DiffAssemble model, when fine-tuned on TEXMET, achieves 85.89% puzzle accuracy on textile data. Most significantly, applying this fine-tuned model to 45 highly degraded Oseberg fragments yields a performance increase from 26.67% to 64.44% puzzle accuracy. These results establish TEXMET as a new benchmark for textile reconstruction and demonstrate that domain adaptation is essential for applying puzzle-solving methods to imagery of real archaeological materials.

VISAGE: Vertex-based Intrinsic Surface Appearance GEneration via Dual-Stream Diffusion for Arbitrary 3D Heads
Jeremias Krauß, Pragati Jaiswal, René Schuster, Didier Stricker

cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Recovering identity-faithful, physically based surface appearance for 3D human heads from sparse images remains challenging. Existing methods often rely on UV-based pipelines, introducing seam artefacts, or operate in image space, limiting surface consistency. We present VISAGE, Vertex-based Intrinsic Surface Appearance GEneration, a surface-based conditional diffusion framework that synthesises per-vertex albedo and roughness directly on arbitrary mesh topologies, without requiring consistent UV parameterisation at training time. VISAGE combines global spectral processing with local geodesic K-Nearest-Neighbour refinement, enforcing structural coherence while restoring detail beyond the truncated spectral basis. Multi-view RGB images condition the network via visibility-aware cross-attention, enabling occlusion-robust synthesis across the full head surface. Region-specific modules specialise in semantically demanding and distinct areas (eyes, mouth, skin and hair), while a tangent-space output projection recovers fine iris detail. We further introduce a scalable procedural augmentation pipeline that enriches raw geometry scans with physically plausible albedo, roughness and normal maps for training. The predicted attributes render directly as a single RGBA vertex-colour channel. VISAGE reduces FID by 42.7% on NPHM and 22.5% and 4.4% on zero-shot FaceVerse and i3DMM, respectively, relative to the strongest baseline, with a 7.2M-parameter core network.

查看完整摘要
Spatiotemporal scene reconstruction with Dynamic Gaussian Splatting (DGS) fundamentally depends on perfect temporal synchronization across all cameras, an assumption rarely achievable in practice. Industry-standard synchronization requires expensive specialized hardware and complex setup, while data-driven alternatives including audio-based alignment, geometry matching, and pose tracking remain limited by restrictive environmental or scene-specific assumptions. To overcome these limitations, we propose Phase-DGS, a framework that replaces conventional timestamps with a semantic phase representing each frame's inherent state within the underlying motion cycle. Frames capturing the same motion state share the same phase regardless of which camera records them or when, naturally establishing spatiotemporal correspondence across unsynchronized cameras. We validate Phase-DGS under temporal offsets, frame drops, and camera freezes, achieving up to +8.5 dB PSNR improvement over baselines while enabling seamless integration with existing DGS backbones including RealTime-4DGS and FreeTimeGS.

We introduce BhopalMLS, a large-scale Mobile Laser Scanning (MLS) dataset from a dense, heterogeneous urban environment in Bhopal, India, with mixed traffic, informal structures, and narrow unplanned streets. Existing 3D datasets, collected in well-planned cities, are structurally more uniform than such unplanned settings, and lack their subtype diversity. Video character replacement aims to replace actors while preserving facial expressions, motions, and scene dynamics. Existing methods typically rely on masked generation with pose guidance, limiting fine-grained expression and motion preservation in complex scenes. We propose ReXMo, an end-to-end framework that supports single-, multi-, and selective character replacement scenarios. ReXMo introduces paired triplet supervision, where synthesized driving videos and original videos share identical performances but different identities, enabling scalable training through synthetic data generation. An In-Context Diffusion Transformer learns identity transformation by jointly conditioning on driving videos, reference identities, and text prompts, while an Expression Enhancement Adapter improves facial expression preservation in multi-person scenes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ReXMo outperforms existing methods in identity fidelity, motion consistency, expression preservation, and visual quality. Our dataset and code will be publicly released.

A key challenge in colon capsule endoscopy (CCE) analysis is accurately identifying whether multiple images depict the same polyp, which is critical to avoid over-counting and unnecessary clinical interventions. Additionally, this prevents prolonged follow-up colonoscopies where endoscopists search for duplicate polyps without precise localization. This study presents a novel contrastive learning-based approach to polyp matching using a Siamese neural network architecture. The model was trained on a dataset from the Danish CareForColon2015 trial. These results suggest that automated polyp matching has the potential to reduce polyp over-counting, improve patient risk stratification, and reduce unnecessary follow-up colonoscopies.

Reconstructing three-dimensional (3D) coronary anatomy from sparse angiographic views could support percutaneous coronary intervention, but limited angular coverage and imperfect vessel segmentations introduce substantial geometric ambiguity. We propose a vessel-specific reconstruction framework combining a learned 3D diffusion prior, visual-hull initialization, and projection-based data consistency. The unconditional diffusion model is trained on coronary vessel masks without paired 2D--3D data and reused across different view counts without retraining. During inference, a scaled visual hull initializes reconstruction, while projection-based updates constrain diffusion refinement using patient-specific vessel observations. We evaluated the framework on simulated angiograms derived from ImageCAS computed tomography angiography volumes using two, four, and eight views at two contrast levels. Across 50 evaluation coronary-tree cases, the proposed method achieved higher mean Dice and clDice than visual hull, deep image prior (DIP), and hash-grid implicit neural representation (INR) at all view counts under reduced contrast. With eight views, improvements over DIP, the best-performing baseline at lower contrast, were approximately 0.13 in Dice and 0.22 in clDice. At higher contrast, performance remained competitive with hash-grid INR, the best-performing eight-view baseline. The preferred diffusion starting timestep depended on observation quality and view count, with excessive refinement reducing accuracy at higher contrast. Projection guidance improved both metrics across all conditions. Averaging stochastic reconstructions further improved accuracy, while sample disagreement localized errors with average-precision gains of 0.14--0.21 over error prevalence. These findings support diffusion-prior reconstruction from sparse vessel observations, although clinical validation remains necessary.

cs.CVcs.MM
查看完整摘要
Coarse-to-fine image-to-point-cloud (I2P) registration risks discarding correct but ambiguous patch pairs, while retaining more candidates repeats fine matching over overlapping patches. SparseGraph-I2P aims to improve registration recall by increasing the inlier ratio at the pose solver, using sensor or monocular depth. A Coarse Hypothesis Graph combines appearance with depth-derived first- and second-order compatibility to select a fixed budget of patch pairs. Sparse Fine Matching decodes each queried pixel once and skips pixel--point comparisons covered by an ancestor patch paired with the same point-cloud region. With sensor depth, depth-aware filtering ranks correspondences by SC2 spectral support for a single EPnP solve. With monocular depth, depth or inverse depth is calibrated from correspondence geometry before filtering. SparseGraph-I2P achieves the highest registration recall and lowest latency among the compared methods on RGB-D Scenes V2 and 7-Scenes with sensor depth. With predicted depth, it retains the highest recall on RGB-D Scenes V2 at an additional calibration cost. Code will be released.

3D semantic scene completion plays a critical role in enabling various downstream tasks within autonomous systems by estimating both missing geometric structures and semantic information in scene data. In this work, we introduce a unique neural framework that integrates state space modeling with diffusion-based generative approaches to enhance 3D point cloud semantic scene completion. We propose \textit{MPath Mamba} diffusion network, leveraging a skip-triple Mamba structure, dimensional decomposition residuals, and multi-directional dilations within multi-path data to efficiently process long-sequence data for feature extraction. 2026-11-03 09:12:46 | arXiv:2611.04548v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Synthetic Aperture Imaging (SAI) enables drones to observe forest canopies by warping and averaging multiple displaced views at a common depth. However, occlusion is suppressed rather than eliminated, causing poorly observed regions to appear occupied and leading to systematic biomass overestimation. The Deep Forest project cannot distinguish reconstruction artifacts from real vegetation because its training data relies on measured reflectance points, excluding empty space and preventing the network from learning explicit occupancy boundaries. This thesis addresses the problem by modifying training labels rather than the imaging process. Each simulated scene is rendered twice: as a 9x9 grid of top-down captures for focal-stack generation and as 550 depth-clipped slabs derived from scene geometry to provide occupancy labels, explicitly representing void space as a negative class. A Gazebo pipeline generates procedural forest stands containing 80-200 trees across three species. A pretrained Pix2Pix model translates rendered RGB images into a synthetic near-infrared band, and Light Field Rendering produces aligned focal stacks of 550 planes across three channels. Four architectures were evaluated on a held-out scene. The plain 2D U-Net achieved an IoU of 0.6290, outperforming the volumetric 3D U-Net (0.5707). Established reconstruction methods performed substantially worse: COLMAP achieved 0.3446 and DepthAnything3 0.2959, both after their respective optimal dilation sweeps. An RGB-input variant of the volumetric architecture achieved the highest IoU of 0.6514 without dilation or per-scene calibration. These results highlight the limitations of conventional reconstruction methods, which recover visible surfaces but struggle to infer occupancy within closed canopies.

Radar-based human sensing typically relies on end-to-end task-specific models trained on hand-chosen signal projections, which generalize poorly across unseen users, tasks, and hardware. We argue that the dominant signal processing choice, the micro-Doppler spectrogram, is a poor starting point for transfer because it keeps the Doppler axis, which carrier frequency rescales, and discards the angular axis, which carrier frequency leaves unchanged. We instead pretrain a convolutional beta-VAE without labels on clutter-removed raw radar measurements from four platforms spanning 60 and 77 GHz and 4 to 12 virtual antennas, aligned by a unified spectral interpolation grid. For every downstream benchmark the encoder stays frozen, and only a small supervised temporal classifier is trained on its embeddings. We compare against supervised models trained end to end on each benchmark and a pretrained radar foundation model across ten settings covering activity, gesture, sign-language, and gait recognition. When the test users and the radar are seen during training, our encoder is best on all five benchmarks. For users never seen in training, it leads on three of four benchmarks, by 4.8 to 23.2 points. On subject-disjoint gait recognition, it is best for each of the four held-out subjects and 8.3 points above the strongest baseline on average, even though its pretraining excluded every recording of this radar. Excluding the test radar from pretraining costs at most 5.3 points on any benchmark. Code is available at https://github.com/jonasmueler/UniversalRadarEncoder_WACV.

Recent advances in generative models have made synthetic images increasingly photorealistic, posing growing challenges to reliable and generalizable detection of AI-generated images. Existing methods typically rely on holistic image-level classification, which may overlook local artifacts, especially in high-resolution images. To address this limitation, we formulate this task as a falsification-driven multiple instance learning problem, where a single fake patch can falsify the whole image, while a real image requires all patches to be authentic. Based on this formulation, we propose \textit{Learning to Falsify (LTF)}, a hierarchical multiple instance learning framework that models an image as multiple bags of instances across different spatial levels and aggregates their predictions under the falsification-inspired principle. We further introduce Cross-Shuffle during training to encourage the model to learn local artifact evidence rather than image-level shortcuts. Furthermore, we propose the \textit{Falsification-driven Test-time Aggregation Strategy (FATAS)}, a plug-and-play module that extends the same falsification-inspired principle to existing detectors by aggregating patch-level predictions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LTF achieves strong generalization and robustness across multiple benchmarks, while FATAS substantially improves representative detectors in high-resolution scenarios without retraining. Code is available at https://github.com/HuiGuanLab/LTF.git

3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables real-time novel view synthesis (NVS), but its heavy storage makes compression essential, and both training and compression introduce representation-specific distortions. Since generative compression in many methods treats geometric and color attributes via different parameters, it can induce dimension-specific distortions, motivating multi-dimensional assessment. Moreover, real-world captures generally lack an aligned ground-truth video for a synthesized, continuous camera trajectory, leaving only sparse captured views as references. To enable multi-dimensional quality assessment of 3DGS videos with scarce aligned references, we first present 3DGS-VBench+, a large-scale subjective video quality assessment (VQA) benchmark comprising 3{,}036 3DGS reconstructions, each paired with a rendered video, across 11 real-world and 22 synthetic scenes, generated by 6 representative compression algorithms at systematically designed levels and annotated with 9{,}108 MOSs covering overall, geometry, and color quality. Based on 3DGS-VBench+, we propose 3DGSV-Assessor, a reference-flexible, large multimodal framework that scores all three dimensions in one generation pass and fuses a ranking-based reference cue into the frozen regressor through a bounded gate, supporting both reduced-reference (RR) and no-reference (NR) assessment. Extensive experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art correlation with human perception on 3DGS-VBench+ and strong zero-shot generalization to other NVS benchmarks. The dataset and code will be released at https://github.com/YukeXing/3DGSV-Assessor.
